One that said `pending` or `cancelled` stays so until a person says otherwise: those are said of the contract, and no calendar brings a cancelled contract back. Decided on 2026-11-14, when the second review of 0.6.0 showed that a term entered early for a contract that had expired began and covered nothing. (Was P-109.) |
| D-156 | A date that is given to a coverage and is the contract's own is kept as blank, also when a person gives an existing coverage the last day of its contract as its end: the coverage then follows the contract into a renewal. An item that is to be left out of a renewal has its coverage ended on an earlier day, or is taken out of the contract once that is renewed. Decided on 2026-11-14 as built, with the cost known: "this device ends with this term" is not said by giving the last day of the term. (Was P-110.) |
| D-157 | Four releases come between 0.6.0 and the MVP, each with a row in the build plan and its objectives under "From 0.6.1 to 0.9.0" there. **0.6.1** carries the corrections to 0.6.0 and adds no scenario. **0.7.0** makes renewals workable, with a worklist, a coverage marked as not to be renewed and the contacts of contracts as contact records; takes the capacity record over time, the multiplying line and the report by region or site; and gives a location and a catalog entry one link or more, each an address and its text, such as the web page of a location or the data sheet of a model. **0.8.0** adds a first tracking of telecommunication circuits beside the inventory: the devices each connects, its A side and Z side locations, its primary carrier and additional carriers such as a local exchange carrier or the provider of a co-location cross-connect, circuit numbers, the billing account number and the reference of the billing contract, the number and address of technical support, the carrier's account team for escalation, the site contacts of both sides, monthly recurring cost by line, and its owner. **0.9.0** adds reference files: photographs, maps, sketches and floor plans for a location, and documents for a catalog entry such as an administration guide, uploaded, attached or linked, and served by `hirdir` itself where a closed network has no internet access. The requirements, the data model and the interfaces of each are written when it is started, and what they leave open is proposed then. R14.7 and R14.8, which were to follow the MVP, are in 0.7.0. Decided on 2026-11-14. |
| D-158 | Additions to the circuits of 0.8.0 (D-157). A circuit has a name, a description and notes, and the enterprise's own circuit ID, assigned locally, as equipment has a property tag beside its serial number: not every circuit has a carrier's circuit number, dark fiber that a business partner provides between two buildings has none, and every circuit of the enterprise can have an enterprise circuit ID. A carrier's circuit number is therefore optional. A carrier bills a circuit as a monthly recurring cost, and that is what is entered; the circuit and the report of circuits show the annual cost, as support agreements, renewals and support costs are tracked by the year. The enterprise circuit ID is unique where it is given, as an asset tag is. Decided on 2026-11-14. |
| D-159 | Each field that a contract may have from its agreement says, beside the value in force and its source, what the agreement gives for it: `effective.<field>.from_agreement`, and for the reseller `from_agreement_label` with it. It is the agreement's value in force today, whether the contract overrides it or not, and blank for a contract in no agreement or where the agreement has none. A contract whose own value is the same as the agreement's still overrides it. Decided on 2026-11-14 as it stood. (Was P-111.) |
| D-160 | An earlier term of a contract, in `GET /contracts/{hid}/terms`, has the dates it had in force: where the contract had none of its own, the dates of its agreement on the last day of that term, read from the agreement's audit trail as it stood then. *(The day was replaced by D-163: the agreement's term in force when the contract's term began.)* Each date says where it came from, `starts_on_source` and `ends_on_source`, `own`, `agreement` or `none`, and the term names the agreement it was in, `agreement_hid`. A renewal of the agreement is not a term of the contracts co-termed with it: it is a term of the agreement. **An agreement has a purchase history too**: `GET /agreements/{hid}/terms` lists the terms it had before, each with its number, its period, its cost, its purchase reference and when the renewal that ended it was entered and by whom, read from the audit trail in the same way, and the agreement's page shows them as a contract's page shows its own. Decided on 2026-11-14, changed by the answer, which added the history of an agreement. (Was P-112.) |
| D-161 | An item that is on a purchase line is of the line's model. An item that is put on a line and names no model, by hand or by a file, takes the line's; one that names another is refused with `422` on `catalog_entry_hid`, which says the line's model. The model of an item on a line is not changed to another while it is there: it is taken off the line first. What 0.6.0 let in is left as it is and is not held against an edit of anything else. **It is reported, to be put right**: `purchase_line_mismatch` on the record of an item and on the item list is true where the item is on a line and of another model, and each purchase line counts its own, `mismatched_items`, which leads to them in the grid. Decided on 2026-11-14, changed by the answer, which asked for what 0.6.0 let in to be reported. (Was P-113.) |
| D-162 | The contracts that still count an item which is out of service are `out_of_service_contracts` on its record, each a contract reference, and `out_of_service_contract.number` and `out_of_service_contract.hid` on the item list, to filter and sort by, as `contract.number` and `contract.hid` are for the contracts that cover it today. In a file of items they are the column `out_of_service_contracts`, which is not loaded. A contract counts the item when it covers it past today, a coverage that ends today asking nothing more of the vendor, or when its coverage was ended because the item left and the term of the contract still runs (`left_service`). They are blank for an item in service. Decided on 2026-11-14 as it stood, and its wording was put right the same day, after the review, to say what 0.6.0's coverage report already did: "past today". (Was P-114.) |
| D-163 | **Which term of its agreement an earlier term of a contract takes its dates from.** A contract with no dates of its own is a member of its agreement and shares its period, and its number belongs to a term of the agreement: an earlier term of such a contract has the dates of the agreement's term that was in force when the contract's term began, on the day it was created or the day its previous renewal took effect, read from the agreement's audit trail as the agreement stood then. So it shows the period it belonged to whichever was renewed first, the agreement or the contract. A contract that is linked to an agreement and has a period of its own, for discounts and tracking, has its own dates as overrides, and its terms show them; one that is not linked, and ends on the same day by coincidence, stands by itself and is renewed by itself. Replaces the day that D-160 named, "the last day of that term", which gave the agreement's new period to a contract renumbered after the agreement was renewed, and replayed the agreement to the wrong moment. Decided on 2026-11-14, after the review of 0.6.1's server, from the owner's description of the three ways a contract stands to an agreement. **What it does not yet date right**, as the second review showed and the owner chose to leave to 0.7.0 (D-164): a member that is renumbered before the agreement's new term has begun, whose new number then began under the old term and later shows the old period too; and a contract that joined or left the agreement partway through a term, whose earlier term takes the agreement term in force when it began, or nothing. |
| D-164 | **A renewal of an agreement may renumber its members, in 0.7.0.** Where the contracts of an agreement share its period, a renewal is a replacement of the old numbers with new ones: renewing the agreement takes, for each member contract, its new number, in the same change, so that each contract's history shows the old number with the agreement's old period. Each new number waits as the member's next term and begins with the agreement's new term, so that it is dated by the term it belongs to; and an earlier term of a contract that joined or left the agreement partway through is dated by the days it was a member. Until then, the agreement is renewed and each member is given its new number by a renewal of its own, and D-163 dates the earlier terms, with the limits it names. Decided on 2026-11-14, for 0.7.0 beside the renewal worklist, and extended the same day by the answer to the second review: the dating of earlier terms is put right there, and not in 0.6.1. |
| D-165 | **How the response target is measured.** A sorted, filtered page of the item grid meets the target when, on the inventory that `make fixture` makes and an otherwise idle reference host, the median of 15 requests after 2 warm-ups is under 300 ms and the 95th percentile under 600 ms, twice the target, so that the slow tail is bounded too. `make time-filters` measures it. Decided on 2026-11-14, with the timing of 0.6.1, the 95th percentile's bound added by the answer. |
| D-166 | **The coverage report and the coverage filters, worked out once.** The report is counted in one pass: one scan of the items tallies every section, where 0.6.1 runs a count for each. What is in force for each contract, its term, its dates, its status and its number, is worked out once for a request, in a table of that request's own, and every item looks it up, where 0.6.1 works it out again for each item. The report's answer is kept and given again until it could differ: it is kept with the newest audit event, the day in the application's time zone and what was asked, filter and all, and any change, which always writes an audit event, or the turn of the day leaves it unused. What is kept is no record: it may be thrown away at any time and is worked out again from the records, so nothing inherited is copied down. A report that is worked out anew shows its counts first and each list when it is opened. The report took 0.8 s at 50,000 items in 0.6.1, and nothing in it changes but by a change or by the day. Agreed in principle by the owner on 2026-11-15; how close to 300 ms (D-165) it comes is measured on the fixture. **Measured at 0.7.0:** one pass over the items took three times as long as a count of each section, which the report now does (D-184); a kept answer takes some 13 ms, and one worked out anew 1.4 s, the rest left to the timing of 1.0.0. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-115.) |
| D-167 | **An import is applied in the background, in batches.** The file is uploaded and previewed as now. Applying it starts a job of the server that runs beside the requests: the page shows how far it has got, and the person may leave it and come back, or go on working. The job applies a few dozen rows at a time, each batch a transaction of its own with its audit events, so that a sign-in or another change waits a fraction of a second behind it at most. A failure partway leaves the batches already applied in place, the job says which rows were applied and which were not, and it is resumed from where it stopped. A row whose record was changed by somebody else after the preview is not applied, and is reported, while the rest go on. This replaces "an apply is one transaction" of D-67, and changes what scenario S15 expects, which are settled with the particulars when 0.7.0 is started. One transaction holds the one writer of SQLite for about five seconds for each thousand rows: a sign-in beside an apply of a thousand rows was answered Busy in 0.6.1, and an apply of ten thousand would stop every change for about fifty seconds. A job in another process alone would not help: SQLite has one writer for all processes. Agreed in principle by the owner on 2026-11-15, with what it costs: a large import is no longer all or nothing. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-116.) |
| D-168 | **A renewal alert after sign-in, and the count of lapsed contracts by the filter.** A setting of the site, `renewal_warning_days`, 90 unless an Admin changes it, says how far ahead a contract counts as ending soon: the coverage report's list of what runs out soon, the renewal worklist and the alert take it, where 0.6.1 holds 90 in the code. After signing in, a person with the Inventory or the Admin role is shown how many contracts have lapsed, how many have a renewal pending, and how many end within the warning period, each count leading to those contracts on the worklist. It is shown once: a person who has seen it is not shown it again until a contract comes onto one of the three lists that was not on it when they saw it. What each person has seen is kept on the server, so that another browser does not show it again, and keeping it is a change of their own, audited. Nothing is written to a contract: what has lapsed or ends soon is worked out when it is read, and no clock writes anything. The day's summary is worked out in advance, just after midnight in the application's time zone and when the server starts, and kept as D-166 keeps the coverage report, so that the first sign-in of the day is not kept waiting. The coverage report's count of lapsed contracts follows its filter: it counts the lapsed contracts that cover at least one item in the view, and an unfiltered report still counts them all. The count of lapsed contracts in 0.6.1 was of the whole inventory, whatever the report was narrowed to. The owner asked to be told shortly after signing in of what needs renewing, with a warning period that the site can change. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15: the alert for Inventory and Admin only, once until something new, and the count by the filter. **Changed by the owner the same day, after the review of 0.7.0: shown once a day to each person, and of the agreements as well (D-179).** Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-117.) |
| D-169 | **A device that is not to be renewed.** A catalog entry and an item carry a flag that says the coverage is not to be renewed, and an item inherits it from its model, with its source, as it inherits the other values of the catalog: set once on a model of access point, every item of that model has it, and one item may override it, so that this access point is renewed while the others of its model are not. It is meant for gear whose support came with its purchase for a first period and is not bought again, such as wireless access points and telephones of a low price, for which the organization keeps spares to replace one that fails. A renewal of a contract leaves a flagged item behind: its coverage ends with the present term. A flagged item in service with no coverage is no gap: it is left off the coverage report's list of what no contract covers, and is counted by itself. A list of the flagged items, filtered, sorted and exported as the item list is, and a count of them by model, show what has been left out of support on purpose. The flag is set and cleared by hand, by a bulk edit and by a file, audited and role-checked as any change. This replaces the mark on one coverage that the build plan named for 0.7.0. Some gear is not worth covering after the support that came with it ends, and its lack of cover is a choice and not a gap. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15, with the flag on the model as well as on the device. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-118.) |
| D-170 | **More than one account manager and support contact.** An agreement and a contract may name any number of account managers and of support contacts, each a contact record as 0.7.0 makes them, beside the free text that stays possible. A contract that names none of its own has those of its agreement, with their source; one that names any has its own, all of them, in place of the agreement's. A vendor's account team is often more than one person, a manager and a backup. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-119.) |
| D-171 | **A mistake on a purchase line is put right.** An operator with the Inventory role may correct the model of a line at any time, a typing error or a wrong model on the request among them, and not only until something has arrived on it, as 0.6.0 had it. Where items have arrived on the line, the correction asks whether they are to have the corrected model as well: yes changes them in the same change, each with its audit event; no leaves them as they are, and each is then marked as of another model than its line's (`purchase_line_mismatch`), to be looked at one by one. The browser test of that mark, which changes the server's answer in the page because no request can make such an item, stays as it is. The data of procurement is typed by people who do not deal in network part numbers every day, and a line is wrong now and then. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15, with the question about the items that have arrived. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-120.) |
| D-172 | **Lines that this inventory does not track.** A request bought through procurement holds lines of what this inventory does not follow beside the network equipment it does: cable ties, hook-and-loop tape, monitors, printers. A line keeps the part number and the description as the procurement system gave them, and the model of the catalog it is matched to, which may be none. A line with no model is not tracked: it is listed with its request, takes no item, is in no count, and does not keep the request from being received in full. A file of a request's lines matches each part number to the catalog, and a line whose part number matches no model is not tracked at once; an import never makes a catalog entry from a line, as a file of items does (P-14). An operator may match a line that is not tracked to a model at any time, which makes it tracked, or take a model off a line on which nothing has arrived. How a part number is matched, by the PID alone or with the vendor, and what one that answers to two models does, are settled when 0.7.0 is started. Most of what a request buys is not network equipment, and a request is often loaded whole from procurement. The owner chose that a line which matches nothing is not tracked at once, knowing that a mistyped switch is then not tracked until somebody matches it. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-121.) |
| D-173 | **What a renewal of an agreement does with each member (D-164).** The renewal lists every contract that shares the agreement's period, each with a choice: a new number, the same number, or ends with this term. The same number is the default, as a co-termed contract follows its agreement today. A new number waits as the member's next term and begins with the agreement's new term (D-164). A member that ends with this term keeps the dates of the present term and leaves the agreement when the term ends, so that the new term does not carry it, and its coverage ends with it. It is one change of the agreement and of each member, audited. Of the contracts of one agreement, the vendor gives some new numbers, carries some over, and some are dropped, as when a site is closed. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-122.) |
| D-174 | **The history of a contract that joins or leaves an agreement.** Joining an agreement, or leaving one, starts a new line in the history of a contract, as a renewal does. Each line says the number, the period in force in that part, its own or the agreement's with where each date came from, the agreement it was in or that it stood by itself, and the days of the part: from when it began, by a renewal, by joining or by leaving, until it ended. So CON-7, alone from January to the end of June and in the agreement from July, has two lines, each with the period it had then. One line could only show one of the two periods, and either would be wrong for half the year. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15; it is the particular of what D-164 says of a contract that joined or left partway through. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-123.) |
| D-175 | **Other values of support that a model gives and a device overrides.** The support that a device needs is said on its model and inherited by each device of it, which may override it: for example the support level required, 24x7 with four-hour replacement for a few C9300-48S switches while most of that model need 8x5 next business day. Such a requirement could then be held against the support level of the contract that covers the device. Not scheduled: it is written down so that the flag of D-169 is built in a way that more such values can follow. The owner named it on 2026-11-15 as a value of the same kind as the flag not to renew. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as an idea that stands for later: nothing of it is built. (Was P-124.) |
| D-176 | **When a pending renewal is overdue.** A setting of the site, `renewal_pending_overdue_days`, 60 unless an Admin changes it, says how long a contract may say that its renewal is pending before it is flagged as overdue: on the renewal worklist, in the alert after sign-in (D-168) and in the coverage report. It is counted from the end of the term the renewal is for. Procurement takes thirty to ninety days after a term has ended, and a renewal that waits longer wants somebody to ask why. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-125.) |
| D-177 | **What a site may adjust is a setting, with a default built in.** A value of a business rule, or one that people see, is not held in the code where a site could reasonably want another: the warning period of renewals, the days after which a pending renewal is overdue, how far ahead the end-of-life report looks, how many items one request may cover, and the like. Each has a default and a description in the application, which are used where the site has said nothing. Two places hold them, as now: what people change in the course of work is a setting of the inventory, in the database, changed by an Admin in the web UI, by the API or by the command line, audited, and carried in a backup; what belongs to the host and the service, the address it listens on, the database, the length of a session, the backups, is in `/etc/hirdir/config.toml`. A command writes a complete configuration file from the application itself, every key with its default and a description, so that an Admin can start again from it rather than keep a copy of the file the package or an installer put there; and the settings of the inventory are listed with their defaults, their present values and their descriptions, each returned to its default by one change. A limit that protects the service, such as the length of a pattern in a filter or the size of a page, may stay in the code, and is then listed with the rest and said to be fixed. When 0.7.0 is started, every such value in the code is listed and each is made a setting or said to be fixed. A value that a site would change should not need a new release; and a configuration file that the application writes is always complete and current. Agreed by the owner on 2026-11-15. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-126.) |
| D-178 | **The particulars of the renewal worklist.** `GET /reports/renewals` is of today, and takes `filter`, `include_drafts` and `include_archived` as `GET /contracts` does, and `within_days`, which is the `renewal_warning_days` setting unless asked otherwise. It has five lists of contracts and one of items. `ending`: the contracts that cover today and whose term in force ends from today to `within_days` ahead, with no renewal entered that is still to begin, their own or, for one whose end is its agreement's, the agreement's, and that the team has not marked not to renew. `lapsed`: those of D-140. `renewal_pending`: those whose status in force is `renewal_pending`, each with `renewal_pending_days`, the days since the end of the term the renewal is for, none before that end; and `overdue`, the part of them pending for longer than the `renewal_pending_overdue_days` setting (D-176). `ends_with_term`: the contracts the team marked not to renew (D-180) whose term ends within `within_days` or has ended, until a person says they have expired or retires them, shown so that a person sees what is let go, and never in the alert. `to_review`: the members of an agreement that is active and runs on that still say their renewal is pending or that they have expired, and that the team has not marked not to renew (D-180); a contract the team marked not to renew is on the list of what is let go alone, whatever its status says, and never in the alert. `overlapping`: the items that more than one contract covers today, as the coverage report has them. The answer holds `totals`, `sections`, each with its `count` and the `filter` that lists the same rows on `GET /contracts` or `GET /items`, and `groups`, one for each vendor and agreement that has a contract on a list, each with the count of each list and its filters, a contract that stands by itself under its vendor with no agreement; the vendors by name, and within one the agreements by name and those standing alone last. An agreement is on the lists by itself as well, in `agreements`, by the same tests: renewing one renews every contract that shares its period. `section` asks for a page of one list, as `GET /contracts` or `GET /items` returns it, the soonest end first. `renewal_pending_days` and `renewal_overdue` are fields of the contract and of the contract list, to filter and sort by. `hirdir renewals` prints the worklist, and `--section` one list of it, in every format a list is printed in. The web UI has a Renewals page under Contracts, with the counts first and each list when it is opened. The coverage report's `within_days` is the same setting unless asked otherwise. D-168 and D-176 say what the worklist is for and the build plan what it holds; this says how it is asked for, what each list tests, and where it leads. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-127.) |
| D-179 | **The particulars of the renewal alert.** `GET /renewals/alert` answers for the person signed in: the counts of the contracts that have lapsed, that have a renewal pending, of which overdue, and that end within the warning period, and the same of the agreements, each with the section of the worklist it leads to, and `show`, which is true for a person with the Inventory or the Admin role when any of those counts is more than none and they have not dismissed the alert yet that day, in the application's time zone, and for everybody else always false. `POST /renewals/alert/seen` keeps, for the person who sends it, that they have seen it on that day, with the contracts that were on each list then, and it is an audit event on their own user, `alert_seen`. It is a change of their own: any role may send it, and it changes no contract. The next day it is shown again, whether or not anything has changed. The web UI shows the alert as a banner under the header of every page, after signing in, until it is dismissed, and each count leads to its list on the Renewals page; dismissing it sends `seen`. The answer is kept as the coverage report is (D-184), and is worked out just after midnight in the application's time zone and when the server starts. D-168 said what the alert shows and to whom; this says how it is asked for and how it is kept that a person has seen it. The owner changed it on 2026-11-15, after the review: the alert is shown once a day to each person, where it was shown once until something new came onto a list, and it counts the agreements as well as the contracts. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-128.) |
| D-180 | **The particulars of an agreement renewed with its members (D-173, D-164).** `POST /agreements/{hid}/renew` takes, beside what it takes now, `members`: for each contract that shares the agreement's period, a contract in it that is not retired and whose end is the agreement's, having no end of its own, whatever its start, one of `same`, `new_number` or `ends`, with `number`, and optionally `annual_cost` and `purchase_reference`, for `new_number`. A member left out is `same`, and a contract named that is not a member is refused, naming it; a member marked not to be renewed that the renewal carries into the new term is among those to review. `same` is what 0.6.0 does: the member follows the agreement into its new period under its number. Each member may also be given `status`, which it then has from this change, and which nothing else in the renewal gives it: a new number is no confirmation, and a status moves only when a person says so. `new_number` gives the member its new number as a term that begins with the agreement's new term and has no dates of its own: where the agreement's new term is in force at once, the member is renewed at once, recorded as a renewal of the member; where it is still to begin, the new number waits as the member's next term, `next_with_agreement` on its record, and is in force from the day the agreement's next term is, and that is worked out when it is read, as a next term is. A next term of the agreement that is withdrawn withdraws the numbers that wait on it, in the same change, and a member that is renewed by itself in the meantime has that renewal in place of the one that waited. A member whose number waits keeps its status when the number begins. `ends` gives the member the agreement's present end as its own, and its start where it has none of its own, in the same change, because from then it no longer shares the agreement's period and the new term does not carry it, and marks it `ends_with_term`. The mark is the team's own note that it is not to be renewed, which may not have reached procurement or the vendor, so a renewal may still occur: it changes no date, no status and no coverage by itself, and the team handles what follows by hand. A contract so marked is on no list of the worklist but `ends_with_term`, where it stays from when it ends within the warning period, and also once its period has passed, until a person says it has expired or retires it; it is not lapsed, and it covers nothing past its period, as an active contract does not. The mark is given and taken off by hand, also to a contract that shares its agreement's dates, which then still follows its agreement; it stays through a renewal, and goes when a person takes it off or the contract is taken out of its agreement or moved to another. A member that ends stays grouped in the agreement, with its own dates, so that the agreement's list and the contract's history show it; it is retired once it has ended, as any contract is. The renewal is one change of the agreement and of each member, each audited under one request ID, and its answer lists what it did with each member. **No member's status is changed by the renewal**: a member that says `renewal_pending` or `expired` while its agreement is renewed is flagged to review, `to_review`, because what procurement agreed is often told by email or in a meeting and the team holds no copy of the new agreement to load; the answer of the renewal lists them, the agreement's record counts and lists them, `members_to_review`, and the renewal worklist has a list of them, `to_review`, so that each is made active, taken out of the agreement, or retired by a person. D-173 said what a person chooses for each member; this says how it is sent, how a number waits for the term it belongs to, and what a contract that ends with its term is afterwards. The owner answered on 2026-11-15 that a member that ends stays grouped in the agreement, shown as ended, and is retired by hand later; and, after the review, that no member's status is changed by a renewal of its agreement, and that those still pending or expired are flagged to review in the renewal's answer, on the agreement and on the worklist; and that the mark not to renew is the team's note, which changes nothing by itself, stays through a renewal and goes when it is taken off or the contract leaves its agreement; and that a member is every contract in the agreement whose end is the agreement's, whatever its start; and, after the second review, that a new number changes no status: the operator confirms in the renewal which members move forward, setting them active where that applies, and selects one, some or all of them to do so at once, on the renewal and on the agreement's page. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-129.) |
| D-181 | **The particulars of a contract's history when it joins or leaves an agreement (D-174).** `GET /contracts/{hid}/terms` lists the parts of the contract's history before the present one, the latest first. A part begins when the contract was made, when a renewal took effect, or when it joined, left or moved between agreements, and ends the day before the next part begins. Each part says `began_on` and `ended_on`, the days it lasted in the application's time zone, `began_by`: `created`, `renewal`, `joined` or `left`, a move from one agreement to another being `joined`, the number, the period in force, with where each date came from as D-160 has it, and the agreement it was in, blank where it stood by itself. A part in an agreement with no dates of its own has the dates of the agreement's term in force when the part began (D-163). A part that a join or a leave ended on the day it began lasted no day and is not listed, such as a contract rolled into an agreement on the day it was made; one that a renewal ended on its first day is listed as of that day, so that no number goes missing from the purchase history. The record of a contract says when its present part began, `part_began_on`, and how. So CON-7, alone from January to the end of June and in the agreement from July, has one earlier part, January to June with its own dates, and its present part began on July 1 by joining. D-174 asked for a new line at a join or a leave; this says what each line holds and when it begins and ends. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-130.) |
| D-182 | **The particulars of a device that is not to be renewed (D-169).** A catalog entry has `do_not_renew`, true or false, false unless said, and an item has `do_not_renew`, blank to have its model's, with `effective.do_not_renew` and its source, `own`, `catalog` or `none`, as every value of the catalog is inherited. A minor item has its own model's, as for every other value of the catalog. A row of a file that holds the very value an item has from its model leaves it inheriting (D-65). A renewal of a contract, and of an agreement for each member it carries into the new term, ends the coverage of each item in scope whose flag is in force: the coverage is given the last day of the present term as its own end, in the same change, with an audit event of its own under the renewal's request ID, and the answer counts them, `left_behind`. That end is written on purpose and is kept, where D-156 keeps a date that is the contract's own as blank: it is the one exception, and it is made only by a renewal. The item stays covered to the end of the present term, also for a renewal entered early. An item in service whose flag is in force and that no contract covers today is no gap: it is left off `uncovered` and `expiring` of the coverage report, and is counted in a sixth section, `not_renewed`, with `by_model`, how many there are of each model, its vendor and its PID. `effective.do_not_renew` is a field of the item list and `do_not_renew` of the catalog list, to filter, sort and export by, and both are columns of their files and of a bulk edit. Scenario S42 reads: two items of one model and one of another under one contract; the first model is flagged, and one of its two items is given its own flag of false; the contract is renewed; the flagged item's coverage ends with the old term and the other two follow into the new one, and once its cover has ended the flagged item is on no list of what is uncovered and is counted as not to be renewed. D-169 said what the flag is for; this names its fields, how a renewal leaves an item behind, and how the coverage report counts what is let go. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-131.) |
| D-183 | **The particulars of the contacts of an agreement and of a contract (D-170).** A new record, `contract_contacts`, names a contact for an agreement or for a contract, exactly one of `agreement_hid` and `contract_hid`, in a `role`, `account_manager` or `support_contact`, with a `priority`, the order in which they are listed: left out, one more than the highest there is for that record and role. A contact is named once for one record and one role. It is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was, as a contact assignment is. For each role by itself, a contract that names nobody of its own has the contacts its agreement names, with the source `agreement`; one that names anybody has its own, all of them, in place of the agreement's. The record of an agreement and of a contract answers `contacts`, its own, and `effective.account_managers` and `effective.support_contacts`, each the list in force with its source, and each contact with its name, organization, email address and telephone number as the contact record has them when it is read, a retired one marked so. The free text of `account_manager` and `support_contact` stays beside them and is inherited by itself, as before. The routes are `GET` and `POST /agreements/{hid}/contacts`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /agreements/{hid}/contacts/{link_hid}`, and the same under `/contracts/{hid}`. Files carry `contract_contacts`, loaded after the contracts: a row names an `agreement` by its name or a `contract` by its number, with `vendor` where two vendors use one, a `role`, a `contact` as any column names one, and a `priority`, and is matched by the record, the role and the contact. An import does not remove one. `hirdir admin reset` clears them with the rest of the inventory. Scenario S43 reads: the account manager of an agreement is a contact record and one contract in it names another; each contract shows the contact in force with its source, and a change of the agreement's contact's telephone number shows on the agreement and on the contract that has it from there. D-170 said that there may be several and that they are inherited; this names the record, the routes and the file, and what S43 sees. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-132.) |
| D-184 | **The particulars of the coverage report at speed (D-166).** The report is counted section by section, a count to each, which on the fixture took a third of the time that one pass over the items took when it was tried (the timing of 0.7.0), and what is in force for each contract, its term, its dates, its status and its number, is worked out once for a request, in a table of that request's own, which the coverage fields of the item list use too: that table is not built in 0.7.0, and goes to the timing of 1.0.0 with the numbers. An answer of the coverage report, of the renewal worklist and of the alert is kept in the memory of the server and given again while nothing could have changed it: it is kept with the newest audit event of a change of the inventory or its settings, a sign-in, seeing the alert, a preview and a change of an account leaving it good, the day in the application's time zone and the request whole, filter, view, section, sort and page, and a newer audit event or another day leaves it unused. Every change writes an audit event, a setting's among them, so a kept answer is never one that a change has made wrong. At most 64 answers are kept, the least used dropped first; a restart drops them all, and they are worked out again from the records, so nothing is copied down. The web UI asks for the counts first and for a list when it is opened. The timing of the report is measured on the fixture, as the item list's is, and written into "Timing at scale". D-166 said what is worked out once and what is kept; this says what a kept answer is kept with, how many there are, and where they live. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-133.) |
| D-185 | **The particulars of imports in the background (D-167).** `POST /imports/{hid}/apply`, and the apply of a bulk edit, answer at once with `202` and the job, which is then `applying`: the server applies it in a worker of its own, one job at a time, and a job that is asked to apply while another is being applied waits as `queued`. The rows are worked in the order the file needs, worked out once for the whole file, and applied 50 at a time, each batch a transaction of its own with its audit events, all under the request ID of the apply; between two batches the one writer's place is let go, so a sign-in or another change waits a fraction of a second at most. A batch never splits the rows that are checked together, the members of one group (D-91): they are one batch however many. Each row is worked again as it was previewed: a row whose record is not at the version previewed, or that would no longer do what was previewed, is not applied and is `stale`, with the reason, and the rest go on; a row that rests on a stale one, such as a minor item in an item that was not made, is refused in its turn, `rejected` with its reason. The role of whoever asked for the job is asked of before each batch, and a job whose asker has lost the Inventory role stops. A row that would make a record that somebody made meanwhile is `stale` too. A job resumed after a stop knows again the records its earlier batches made only for a reference. A job is `applied` once every row has been worked, with the counts of what was applied and what was stale. One that stops partway, because of an error nobody foresaw or because the server stopped, keeps the batches that were applied, undoes the one that was not finished, and is `stopped`, with the reason; a job found `applying` when the server starts was stopped by the stop. `POST /imports/{hid}/apply` on a stopped job resumes it from the first row that was not applied, and nothing is applied twice. `GET /imports/{hid}` answers `rows_done` and `row_count` while it works, only one server runs on one state directory, which it holds a lock on, so that a second one cannot take a job the first is applying for stopped; the web UI shows how far it has got and may be left and come back to, and `hirdir import apply` waits for the end and shows the progress, unless `--no-wait`. Each row says what the apply did with it, `applied_outcome`, blank until it is worked. This replaces "an apply is one transaction" of D-67 and "applies only when every row comes out as previewed" of D-69; the rest of both stands. Scenario S15 reads: preview an import, change the record of one of its rows in the web UI, then apply; that row is not applied and is reported as changed since the preview, and every other row is applied. D-167 said that an apply goes on in batches beside other work; this says how big a batch is, what is checked again, what a stop leaves, and how it is resumed. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-134.) |
| D-186 | **The particulars of purchase lines as procurement has them (D-171, D-172).** A line has `part_number` and `description`, as the procurement system gave them, beside `catalog_entry_hid`, which is now optional; it has one of a model and a part number at least, and `tracked` is true where it has a model. A line that is not tracked takes no item, by a receipt, by hand or by a file, is in no count, has no `received_quantity` that counts, and is not waited for: a request is `received` in full once every tracked line has arrived. `GET /purchase-lines` lists every line, with `tracked` to filter by. `PATCH /purchase-lines/{hid}` may give a line another model at any time; where items have arrived on it, the change names `items`, `follow` or `stay`, and is refused without it. `follow` gives every item on the line that is not retired the corrected model, in the same change, each with its audit event; `stay` leaves them, and they are then marked as of another model than their line's (D-161). A line's model is taken off, making it untracked, only while nothing has arrived on it. In a file of lines, `part_number` is matched to the catalog by the PID, without regard to case or surrounding space, exactly first and, only where nothing matches exactly, without the `=` that ends the part number of a spare, on either side, so that `PWR-C1-715WAC-P=` finds the model `PWR-C1-715WAC-P` and `C9300-NM-8X` its own model beside `C9300-NM-8X=`, among the models of `vendor` where the row gives one and among all models where it does not. One model that answers is the line's; none leaves the line untracked, with a note; two or more, such as one PID of two vendors, leave it untracked, with a note that names them, and never a guess. A row that names `vendor` and `pid` as before is matched in the same way, and a file of lines makes no catalog entry. A row whose part number is the line's as it stands leaves the line's model as it is, so that loading procurement's file again does not undo a model a person corrected; a row whose part number matches nothing leaves a model that a line has been given by hand; a row that would give a line on which items have arrived another model is rejected, to be corrected by hand where the operator can say what becomes of the items. D-171 and D-172 said what is to be possible; this says how a line is matched, what is asked of a correction, and what a file does to a line that a person has matched. The owner answered on 2026-11-15: as proposed, and a spare's trailing `=` is not looked at; and, when the review showed a model and its spare twin both in the catalog, that an exact part number finds its own model first, the `=` being left out of the match only where nothing matches exactly; and that a reload of the same part number leaves a corrected model alone. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-135.) |
| D-187 | **The particulars of settings rather than constants (D-177).** Every value of a business rule held in the code was listed when 0.7.0 was started. Five become settings of the inventory, each with its default and description: `renewal_warning_days`, 90, how far ahead a contract counts as ending soon, for the worklist, the alert and the coverage report's `expiring` (D-168); `renewal_pending_overdue_days`, 60, how long a renewal may be pending before it is overdue (D-176); `end_of_life_within_days`, 730, how far ahead the end-of-life report counts a date as approaching; `end_of_life_date`, `ldos`, which of the four dates it is of unless asked; and `max_items_per_request`, 1000, how many items one request may cover, add to a group or receive, from 100 to 5,000, which the web UI and the command line read and send their pieces by. Each number of days is from 1 to 36,500. What was decided stays in the code, said to be fixed with the decision that fixed it: the states in which an item is out of service (D-142) and leaves its groups and places (D-103), the state an item is received in (D-145), what a condition says of health (D-112), and the review status of what a file makes (P-14). So do the limits that protect the service: the size of a page and of an offset, the length and the parts of a filter and of a pattern, the rows of a file, the depth to which places, groups and minor items nest, the lines of a request, the delay of sign-in, how long an `Idempotency-Key` is kept, the wait for the one writer, and the size of a batch of an import (D-185). `GET /settings` lists the settings with their value, default, description, the values each takes, and whether it is at its default, and then the fixed values, each with `fixed` true, its value, what it limits and why it is fixed; any role reads it, an Admin changes a setting, a fixed value refuses a change, and `PUT /settings/{key}` with `null` returns a setting to its default, as it always has. The web UI's Settings page shows both, with a button that returns a setting to its default. `hirdir admin config` writes a complete configuration file from the application, every key of every section with its default and its description, each commented out at its default, and with `--current` the values the present file sets as they are set; it prints to standard output or writes to `--output`, which it does not overwrite without `--force`. The file the package ships for `hirdir-setup --settings` is written by that command when the package is built, and a test holds the configuration of [architecture.md](architecture.md#configuration) to it, so the three never disagree. `[upgrades] proxy`, which `hirdir-setup` reads, is in it with the rest. A value written in two places, the application's time zone, the five-second wait and the default page among them, is held in one. D-177 said to list every such value and make each a setting or say it is fixed; this is that list, and says how the settings and the configuration file are listed and written. The owner confirmed the list of five on 2026-11-15. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-136.) |
| D-188 | **The particulars of capacity over time (R14.7, R14.8), and the demand of each region.** **A record of the balance**, `capacity_records`, keeps the report as it was in a week: `week_of`, the Monday that begins the week in the application's time zone, `recorded_at`, `note`, and the numbers of `active` and of `with_drafts` as the report answered them, with `weight_per_resource_unit`. There is one record for each week, of the whole installation, as the report is. The server records the whole installation by itself each Monday just after midnight, and when it starts in a week that has none, with `via` of `system`. `POST /capacity-records` records the balance as it is now, by hand, on any day, and replaces the record of that week, with an audit event that holds what it was: so a contractor added on a Wednesday is in that week's point once somebody records again. A person may also correct the figures of a saved week, the `demand` and the `supply` of `active` and of `with_drafts`, by `PATCH /capacity-records/{hid}` with a `note` that says why, which is then required; the record is marked `adjusted`, the balance and the coverage are worked out from the two figures when it is read, and the change is audited. `GET /capacity-records` lists them, oldest first, and the web UI draws the trend as a graph of one point a week, beside the report: demand and supply as two lines with the balance between them shaded, and a switch that stacks the supply by category and splits the demand into the infrastructure and other work. An adjusted week is marked, and shows its corrected totals, with its parts as they were recorded. A record that was a mistake is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was. Scenario S44 records in one week and again in the next, with a line added between them. **A line that multiplies.** A supply line of the category `tools` or `services` has `effect`, `add` unless said, or `multiply`. A line that multiplies has a `factor`, a number of at least 1, a `share`, from 0 to 1, of the positions that use it, 1 unless said, and `applies_to`, the staff lines it works on, which add, and has no `quantity` or `units_each`. Its units are (`factor` − 1) × `share` × the units of the lines it applies to that are counted in the same view on the same day: multipliers work on the base units of staff lines and do not compound. Its units are counted in its own category, so the team's own share stays that of its positions, and the report lists for each multiplying line the lines it works on and the gain. In a file of capacity lines, `applies_to` names the staff lines by name, joined with `;`. **The demand of each region.** The report stays one balance for the whole operations team, since so much of what the team supplies is shared: the tools, DNS, DHCP, network access control, Internet and remote access, and the watch of the NOC. A line's region or site is kept and shown, and narrows nothing. Beside the balance, `demand_by_region` says where the work is: for each region, with its path, the support weight of the items at or beneath it, by their location in force, the infrastructure demand that is, and its share of the whole as a percentage, and a row for the items that are nowhere. A row of a region holds what is beneath it, so the regions at the top and the row of what is nowhere add up to the whole. No supply is divided among regions. Scenario S45 reads: capacity lines and items in two regions; the report is one balance for the whole, and beside it each region's share of the support weight, which with what is nowhere adds up to the whole, and no supply is divided. R14.7 and R14.8 named what is wanted, and D-147 left the report of one place until the report of the whole had been used; this names the records and the fields, and what the report says of the regions. The owner answered on 2026-11-15 that the balance is recorded by itself each Monday and the trend is a graph of one point a week, and that a person may both record again and correct a week's figures; and, asked what the graph shows, two lines of demand and supply with a switch to break each into its parts. The owner also answered that the report stays of the whole operations team, because so much is shared, with the demand of each region shown beside it and no supply divided: the report of one region is not built. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-137.) |
| D-189 | **The particulars of links.** A catalog entry, a region, a site and a location have `links`: a list of up to 20, each an `url` and a `text` to show for it, in the order given. An address begins with `https://` or `http://`, holds no space and is at most 2,000 characters; any other is refused, naming the link. The text is at most 200 characters, holds neither `<` nor `>`, and may be blank, when the address itself is shown. The list is a field of the record, changed whole by `PATCH`, and is audited as one change of it. An item answers `catalog.links`, those of its model, and shows them; a place shows its own and inherits none. In a file the column `links` holds them in order, each written `text <url>` and joined with `;`, and the JSON form the same text, so that a file carries them out and back unchanged. The web UI shows each as a link that opens in a new tab, and gives the page it opens nothing of `hirdir`'s (`rel="noopener noreferrer"`). Scenario S46 reads as the build plan has it. The build plan names the records and says that a site and a region are settled when the release is started; this says what a link is, where it shows, and how a file carries it. The owner answered on 2026-11-15 that sites and regions take links too. Decided on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as it stood and was built. (Was P-138.) |
| D-190 | nginx strips the prefix and names it in `X-Forwarded-Prefix`; the server writes it into the page. Why: Deep links and API documentation need the prefix. This is the `texas2step` way. `sunstone` and `segl` leave the server unaware. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.1.0. (Was P-05.) |
| D-191 | Draft, active and retired are a `record_status` on items, sites and locations from the first release that has them. Planning views come after the MVP. An item that leaves ownership is Released, then Archived, and is shown greyed when asked for; there is no separate dormant store. Why: Leaving drafts out of every count is cheap to build in and costly to add later. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.2.0. (Was P-06.) |
| D-192 | A minor item inherits lifecycle state and disposition from its parent as well as location and owner. Why: A power supply is in production because its switch is. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.4.0. (Was P-07.) |
| D-193 | Asset tag is unique when present. Why: It is used to match import rows. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.2.0. (Was P-08.) |
| D-194 | The session lasts 12 hours and is kept in `localStorage`, so it survives closing the tab. Why: Stood unanswered at its default. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.1.0. (Was P-09.) |
| D-195 | The package is built with `dpkg-deb` in a clean Ubuntu 26.04 container. The service listens on `127.0.0.1:8715`. Why: `dpkg-deb` needs no extra tool. A loopback port is the simplest thing nginx can reach. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.1.0. (Was P-10.) |
| D-196 | Release notes are files in `docs/releases/` and the same text is the GitHub release. Why: They are then published with the rest of the documentation. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.1.0. (Was P-11.) |
| D-197 | The lines-of-code table splits each language into Product, Tests and Tooling by path. Why: It is the table `texas2step` publishes, here produced by `cloc`. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.1.0. (Was P-12.) |
| D-198 | In an import a blank cell leaves the value alone and `!clear` clears it. Why: Blank cells are common in spreadsheets and must not erase data. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.3.0. (Was P-13.) |
| D-199 | A vendor or catalog entry named in an import and not yet known is created and marked unreviewed. Why: Imports should not stall on catalog upkeep. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.3.0. (Was P-14.) |
| D-200 | The same address twice under one network label is accepted and reported. Why: Shared and virtual addresses are real. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.4.0. (Was P-15.) |
| D-201 | Fiscal year starts in January unless the setting says otherwise. Why: Neutral default. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.6.0. (Was P-16.) |
| D-202 | The scale target is 50,000 items. Why: Phones and access points are counted. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood: the target that 1.0.0 is timed against. (Was P-17.) |
| D-203 | Test equipment has an owner and nothing more: no check-out, no calibration dates. Why: Stood unanswered at its default. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood. (Was P-18.) |
| D-204 | Regular expressions use a linear-time engine when its binding has a production release for the Python in use, otherwise a time-limited one. Why: Which one is available changes with the Python version, and the dependency rule decides. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.2.0, with the time-limited `regex` package: RE2's own binding, `google-re2`, has no release for Python 3.15. (Was P-19.) |
| D-205 | A capacity line may be a draft, may carry an annual cost, a vendor, dates and a region or site. The report shows the balance with and without drafts. Why: Drafts already exist for items and sites and give what-if comparisons for free. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.6.0. (Was P-21.) |
| D-206 | `Idempotency-Key` is kept for 24 hours for each caller. The same request with the same key is answered with the first answer and not carried out again; the same key on another request is a `422`. It is taken by a `POST` on an inventory record, an import or a bulk edit, and refused on the routes of accounts. Why: A retry after a lost answer must not create a second record. An account route's answer may hold an access code or a token, which is kept nowhere, so it cannot be kept to be replayed. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.3.0. (Was P-48.) |
| D-207 | `/var/lib/hirdir/backups` is made with mode 0750, by postinst at install and at each upgrade and by `hirdir admin backup` when it is not there. Why: Its mode otherwise depended on the umask of whoever took the first backup. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.3.0. (Was P-58.) |
| D-208 | **A support weight of a place.** A region, a site and a location may carry a `support_weight` of its own, blank unless an enterprise gives one: the work that supporting the network there adds by itself, beside what its devices weigh. Time zones and distance, a language that the team does not speak, buildings spread across a metro area or a campus where another place is one building, a site restricted for national security that needs clearances and allows no cell phones: whatever about the place makes the same devices harder to support. The weight is not inherited by the places beneath: a location divided for reasons of the network, which adds no work, would otherwise count the place's weight once for each part. It is demand: the capacity report adds the weights of the places in the view to the support weight of the items, and `demand_by_region` counts each place's own weight in the row of each region at or above it; a draft place counts with the drafts, a retired one not at all. It is set by hand, by a file of places and by the API, audited and role-checked as any field. Why: The owner asked for it on 2026-11-15, during 0.7.0, for the next release: the burden of supporting a place is not all in its devices, and a team that serves a restricted site or a spread-out campus needs that to show in the capacity balance. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-139.) |
| D-209 | **What a circuit holds beside the list of D-157 and D-158.** `installed_on`, the day the circuit was installed, and `activated_on`, the day it was activated: the carrier usually starts to bill on the activation date, which is often not the install date, so the cost of a circuit counts from its activation. `production`, true or false: whether the circuit carries the enterprise's production work; one that does not is billed by the carrier just as one that does, and the flag says what it is for, not whether it costs. `support_weight`, the work the circuit adds, blank unless given: a circuit with none carries no weight, and one with a weight is demand in the capacity report beside the items and the places (P-139). How the report of cost by year counts a circuit activated partway through a year is settled when 0.8.0 is started. Why: The owner asked for them on 2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, for the circuits of 0.8.0. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-140.) |
| D-210 | **A default and a minimum support weight, for sites, major items and circuits.** Six settings, each blank unless an Admin sets it: `default_site_weight` and `minimum_site_weight`, `default_item_weight` and `minimum_item_weight`, for the major items of the inventory, and `default_circuit_weight` and `minimum_circuit_weight`. A default is had by an object of its kind whose weight is blank; a weight of zero is no blank and stays zero against a default. A minimum raises the weight of an object that has one, its own or the default, to at least the minimum; an object with no weight at all, blank with no default, counts as zero whatever the minimum. So, an object with no weight of its own counts at: 2 with a default of 2 and a minimum of 1; 2 with a default of 1 and a minimum of 2; 0 with no default and a minimum of 1; 1 with a default of 0 and a minimum of 1. A site is a place bounded apart from other sites, in a region or not, holding one location or more; its weight is the one of P-139. An item's weight is had from its model first, as now, so the default is the last level of inheritance, where neither the item nor its model gives one, and is shown with the source `setting`. The owner answered on 2026-11-15: the default is the last level of inheritance, as said. The stack discount is applied after each member's weight is resolved, its own, its model's or the default, raised to the minimum: a stack and a standalone switch start from the same weight, and the stack's weight is the sum of its members' weights with the discount applied to it. The counts show where a weight was raised to the minimum, so that a weight set by policy is told apart from one set by hand. **The capacity report has a what-if, Try**: it takes a default and a minimum of each kind, and other changes to the planning figures, as parameters, and answers the balance they would give beside the balance in force, saving nothing and writing nothing to the audit trail; an Admin saves the values as the settings once the estimate is agreed. A default of a major item by its kind, hardware, virtual machine, software or service, may follow later. Why: The owner asked for it on 2026-11-15, and answered its open points the same day: a few settings give a quick estimate of the support weight before an organization sets weights object by object, and a minimum set by policy keeps the capacity plan honest without asking the team to weigh every object. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-141.) |
| D-211 | **Long reports worked out once a day and on a change, and a check of what is kept.** The capacity balance is kept as the coverage report, the renewal worklist and the summary of the renewal alert are (D-184): given again while nothing could have changed it, to every person and in every session alike, as an answer depends on the records and not on who asks for it. Each of them is worked out just after midnight in the application's time zone, and when the server starts, for the view that its page opens with, so that the first person of the day is not kept waiting; any other view is worked out when it is first asked for, and then kept. An audited change leaves every kept answer unused, as now; narrowing that to the records each answer reads is left to the timing of 1.0.0, if it is needed. A Try of the capacity report (P-141) is worked out each time and never kept. An Admin may check what is kept, with a button on the settings page and its route in the API, since the kept answers are in the server's memory and `hirdir admin` cannot see them: the check works out each kept answer anew, compares it with what was kept, keeps the new one, and answers which of them differed. None should: one that did is a defect, and the check says which report and which view it was. The check is audited with what it found. The route and the shape of the answer are settled when 0.8.0 is started. Why: The owner answered on 2026-11-15, asked whether the capacity balance should be kept as the coverage report is: long calculations once a day and on a change that applies, the result shared across the sessions of every user, and a button for an Admin to force them to be worked out again, which shows whether anything had fallen out of sync. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-142.) |
| D-212 | **A circuit (D-157, D-158, P-140).** A new record, `circuits`, beside the inventory: referenced by HID, audited and role-checked as the inventory is, a draft, active or retired and never deleted, and taking tags. It has `name`, required; `enterprise_circuit_id`, the enterprise's own ID, assigned locally, unique where it is given and without regard to case, as an asset tag is; `description` and `notes`; `circuit_type` and its speed (P-146); its two sides (P-147); `carrier_hid`, the primary carrier or the other party that provides it, a vendor, optional, and `carrier_circuit_number`, the number that carrier gives it, optional, with the other carriers in a record of their own (P-148); `billing_account`, the billing account number, and `billing_contract`, the reference of the billing contract, both text; `installed_on`, the day it was installed, `activated_on`, the day it was activated, from which the carrier bills it, and `disconnected_on`, the last day it was in service, each optional, with no activation before the installation and no disconnection before the activation; `production`, true unless said, since most circuits carry production: whether it carries the enterprise's production work, which changes nothing of what it costs; `support_weight`, blank unless given (P-155); `cost_side` (P-150); and `owner_hid`, an owner as an item has one: the person or team responsible for the circuit, its primary stakeholder, who need not be the one who asked for it. An owner outside the team marks the circuit as an exception, with the owner's reason, as it marks an item. Who pays for a circuit in the enterprise, a sponsoring business unit or a working capital fund, is said by a tag or in the notes, and there is no field of a billing code. The record answers `monthly_cost`, the sum of its cost lines in date today, and `annual_cost`, twelve times that, worked out when it is read and stored nowhere, and lists its carriers, its devices, its cost lines, its groups and the contacts in force. A circuit is **in service** on a day that is not before its `activated_on` and not after its `disconnected_on`, a blank being open, while it is active, or a draft in a view that includes drafts; a retired circuit that has a `disconnected_on` was in service until then, and one retired with none was taken out and is in service on no day. The routes are `/circuits`, `POST /circuits/query` and `POST /circuits/{hid}/retire`; any role reads them and Inventory changes them. `hirdir admin reset` clears circuits, their parts and circuit groups with the rest of the inventory. After the review of the server: a circuit's `monthly_cost` and `annual_cost` are nothing on a day it is not in service, as the report and its groups count it, while its lines keep their amounts; and `in_service` holds for a draft in a view that includes drafts, and for a draft's record read by itself, which shows what it will cost. Why: The build plan names what a circuit holds; this names its fields, the dates that bound what it costs, and who its owner and its payer are. The owner answered on 2026-11-15 that the owner of a circuit is who is responsible for it, its primary stakeholder, and that the payer of a charge-back is marked by a tag or in the notes. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-145.) |
| D-213 | **The type and the speed of a circuit.** `circuit_type` is a value of a new editable list, `circuit_type`, loaded by 0.8.0 with `internet`, `mpls`, `point_to_point`, `dark_fiber`, `wavelength`, `voice` and `other`, and is optional. The speed is three named values, each a number of Mbps with two places and more than zero: `port_mbps`, the speed of the physical interface, and `down_mbps` and `up_mbps`, the rates the circuit is limited to, download and upload. Most commercial circuits run at the full rate of their port both ways and leave the two rates blank; a circuit limited to one rate both ways gives both that rate; broadband gives each its own. A rate is not given without a port speed, and is not more than the port speed. The record answers `effective.down_mbps` and `effective.up_mbps`, the rate in force each way, with the source `own`, or `port` where it is blank and is the port speed, and `speed`, the three written short: `1G` for a port of 1,000 at its full rate, `1G / 200M` for one limited to 200 both ways, `1G / 200M / 20M` for one of 200 down and 20 up, a multiple of 1,000 written in G and a speed below 1 in k. A list filters and sorts by `port_mbps` and by the rates in force, so that `effective.down_mbps:lt:100` finds a full-rate circuit of 50 as well as a limited one. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-15 for a type and a speed, describing the speed as one, two or three values, the port speed first, and asked whether there was a better way to keep it. Of named values and positions, the owner chose named values: nobody has to remember what the second position means, and a limit that is the same both ways is no special case. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-146.) |
| D-214 | **The two sides of a circuit, and the devices it connects.** The A side, the source, and the Z side, the destination, are each a location of the inventory, `a_location_hid` and `z_location_hid`, so that a side has the site, the region, the address and the site contacts its location has, as 0.5.0 resolves them, and nothing is typed again. A side may have no location where that end is not one of the enterprise's places, as the far end of an internet or an MPLS circuit is in the carrier's network: it then has `a_note` or `z_note`, a short text that says where it ends, "carrier network" or "cloud on-ramp", which a side with a location may have too, "demarc in the basement". At least one side of a circuit is a location. A side is not given a location that is retired, and a location is not retired while a circuit that is not retired has a side there. The devices a circuit connects, `circuit_devices`, are items or device groups of the inventory, each on a side, `a` or `z`: the router at each end, or the pair of routers in a group that share it. A device is connected to one circuit once. The record of an item and of a device group lists, in `circuits`, the circuits it is connected to, each with its side, to be read only, and the list of circuits finds those with a side at a location, or at or beneath a site or a region, by `location`. A connection is made by hand, by a file and by the API, and one that was a mistake, or whose device was taken out, is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was. A replacement hands the connections of the item that left to the item that took its place, in the same change, as it hands over its memberships (D-87). A retired item stays listed where it was connected, marked so, and is connected to nothing new. The routes are `GET` and `POST /circuits/{hid}/devices`, and `PATCH` and `DELETE /circuits/{hid}/devices/{link_hid}`. After the review of the server: a replacement hands over the connections of circuits that are not retired, and a retired circuit keeps the device it connected while it ran. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-15 that circuits share what the system already holds, locations and the contact lists of sites among them, and answered that a far end outside the enterprise's network has no location and a note of where it ends, at least one side being a location. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-147.) |
| D-215 | **The carriers of a circuit, and their numbers.** The primary carrier, or the other party that provides the circuit, is `carrier_hid` on the circuit, and its number is `carrier_circuit_number`. The others, any number of them, are `circuit_carriers`: each a vendor, `vendor_hid`, in a `role` of a new editable list, `carrier_role`, loaded with `local_exchange`, a local exchange carrier, `cross_connect`, the provider of a cross-connect in a co-location, `last_mile` and `other`, with `circuit_number`, the number that carrier gives the circuit where it gives one, a `priority`, the order in which they are listed, and `notes`. A carrier is a vendor, from the one list of vendors, so that a company that is a carrier and a reseller is one record, and a business partner who lets the enterprise use its dark fiber is entered as a vendor too. A vendor is named once on a circuit, the primary among them. A carrier's number is unique with that carrier, without regard to case, across every circuit and in both places, as a contract's number is with its vendor; a number is optional, and many a circuit has none. An additional carrier is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was. The list of circuits finds a circuit by the number any of its carriers gives it, `carriers.circuit_number`, and by any of its carriers, `carriers.vendor_hid`. The routes are `GET` and `POST /circuits/{hid}/carriers`, and `PATCH` and `DELETE /circuits/{hid}/carriers/{link_hid}`. Why: D-157 names a primary carrier and any number of others, each with its part, and the circuit numbers they give. The owner asked that circuits share what the system already holds: the carriers are its vendors. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-148.) |
| D-216 | **The contacts of a carrier and of a circuit.** A vendor names the people of its account team and its support desk once, in `vendor_contacts`: a `contact_hid`, a contact record as the contacts of a place and of a contract are, in a `role`, `account_manager`, the account team, whom to go to for escalation, or `support_contact`, the support or repair desk, whose number and address are the telephone and the email of its contact record, with a `priority`, the order in which they are listed. A circuit has those of its primary carrier, for each role by itself, with the source `vendor`, unless it names its own of that role in `circuit_contacts`, which then stand in place of the carrier's, all of them, as a contract's stand in place of its agreement's (D-183). The site contacts of a circuit are those of the locations of its two sides, each role of the `contact_role` list resolved for the location as 0.5.0 resolves it, with the level each came from; a circuit may name its own for a side and a role, `circuit_contacts` with `side` `a` or `z`, which then stand in place of what the location gives for that side and role. A side with no location has only its own. The record of a circuit answers `effective.account_managers` and `effective.support_contacts`, each with its source, and `effective.site_contacts`, for each side each role with its contacts and the level they came from, each contact as its record has it when it is read, a retired one marked so. A contact is named once for one record, side and role, and a naming is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was. Nothing is copied: a new telephone number of a carrier's support desk shows on every circuit of that carrier. The routes are `GET` and `POST /vendors/{hid}/contacts`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /vendors/{hid}/contacts/{link_hid}`, and the same under `/circuits/{hid}`.
