A vendor's page lists its contacts and its circuits. Whether the contracts of a vendor have its contacts too is left until a site asks. Scenario S49 reads: the A side's site has a site contact; the circuit's carrier has an account manager, and a second circuit of that carrier names its own; the circuit shows the site contacts of each side, resolved from its locations with their source, and the carrier's account manager with the source `vendor`, and the second circuit shows its own. Why: D-157 names the carrier's account team for escalation, technical support, and the site contacts of both sides. The owner answered on 2026-11-15 that the account team and the support desk are named once on the carrier itself, and each circuit shows them unless it names its own; and asked that circuits share the contact lists of sites. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-149.) |
| D-217 | **The cost of a circuit, and the side it counts at.** A carrier bills a circuit as a monthly recurring cost of several lines, the port, the local loop, an access charge, a credit, and that is what is entered (D-158): `circuit_costs`, each with `description`, required and unique within its circuit without regard to case, `monthly_cost`, money, which is below zero for a credit, `vendor_hid`, the carrier that bills the line, blank for the circuit's primary carrier, `starts_on` and `ends_on`, optional, the days the line is billed, so that a new rate is a line that ends and one that begins, and `notes`. A line that was a mistake is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was; one that was billed for a time is given an end. A circuit's `monthly_cost` is the sum of its lines in date today, and its `annual_cost` twelve times that. A circuit's cost, and its support weight, count at one site: that of the side that `cost_side` names, `a` or `z`, or of the other side where that one has no location. A new circuit is given `cost_side` from the setting `circuit_cost_side`, `a` unless an Admin makes it `z`; a change of the setting changes no circuit that exists, and any circuit may be given the other side at any time, where the enterprise has chosen to count it at the far end. It is the one value of a circuit that a setting gives once, when the circuit is made, rather than when it is read, because the owner asked that a change of the default apply to new circuits only. The record answers `cost_site_hid`, the site it counts at, with its path. The routes of the lines are `GET` and `POST /circuits/{hid}/costs`, and `PATCH` and `DELETE /circuits/{hid}/costs/{line_hid}`. Why: The owner answered on 2026-11-15: the cost of a circuit is assigned to its A side by default, a circuit can be switched to count at its Z side, and an Admin setting gives the default side, A unless changed, a change of it applying to new circuits only. Its support weight counts at the same side, so that one rule says where a circuit counts and the regions of the capacity report still add up. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-150.) |
| D-218 | **The report of what circuits cost, by year.** `GET /reports/circuit-costs` is of one fiscal year, `fiscal_year`, the one today is in unless asked, which begins on the first day of the month of `fiscal_year_start_month` and is named for the calendar year it ends in, as the end-of-life report has it. It takes `filter` and `include_drafts` as the list of circuits does, and `by`: `carrier`, which it is unless asked, `site`, `region`, `group`, `circuit_type`, `owner` or `tag`. It answers `totals` and `rows`, one for each value of `by`, the blank among them, each with `circuits`, how many were in service on any day of the year, `cost`, what the year costs, `monthly_cost`, what is billed for a month on the day the report is of, today for the present year, the last day of a past one and the first day of one to come, and `annual_cost`, twelve times that. **A part month is counted as the carrier bills it**: each line counts its `monthly_cost` for each month of the year in which it is billed, a whole month in full, and a month that the line and the circuit's service share only in part for the days they share, the monthly cost times those days over the days of the month. So a circuit of 1,000 a month activated on 10 April 2027 costs 8,700 in 2027, May to December in full and 21 of April's 30 days, and one disconnected partway through a month counts that month the same way. Every sum is worked out exactly and rounded to two places once, half up. By `carrier` a line counts under the vendor that bills it, its own or the circuit's primary carrier, and a circuit is in the row of each carrier that bills any of it; by `site` it is in the row of the site it counts at (P-150), and by `region` in that of each region at or above that site, a row of a region holding what is beneath it; by `group` it is in the row of each group it is in, and by `tag` of each tag; the totals count each circuit once. `hirdir report circuit-costs` prints it, with `--year` and `--by`. After the review of the server: by `carrier` a line billed on no day of the year's service puts no carrier in a row, and a circuit with no such line is in its primary carrier's row; a retired group keeps its row, since retiring it ends no membership; and each row's filter lists the circuits the row counted, with the year's window of service and the view it is of, `include_archived` and `include_drafts`, by `billed_by` for a carrier. After the second review: the filter of a carrier's row of the report lists the circuits of the year that carrier provides or bills a line of, whatever the dates of the line, and so may list a circuit more than the row counts where its only line was billed before or after the year: the grammar of filters cannot say a year of a line. Why: P-140 left how the report counts a circuit activated partway through a year to be settled when 0.8.0 was started. The owner answered on 2026-11-15: as the carrier bills it, a part month for its days; and that a circuit counts at the site of the side its cost is assigned to, with groups of circuits to total. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-151.) |
| D-219 | **Groups of circuits.** A collection of circuits that may land at different sites, to be looked at and costed together: the circuits of Internet access, or of a cloud on-ramp such as Azure ExpressRoute. A new record, `circuit_groups`: `name`, required and unique, `description`, `notes`, `owner_hid`, optional, `record_status` and tags. Its members, `circuit_group_members`, are circuits, any number; a circuit may be in more than one group, and is in one group once. A membership is added and removed by hand, by a file and by the API, several at a time; one is removed outright, with an audit event that holds what it was, since a group is a way of looking at circuits and not a fact of their history. A retired circuit stays listed in its groups, marked so, and is added to none; a retired group takes no new circuit. The record of a group answers its circuits, how many of them are in service today, and their `monthly_cost` and `annual_cost`, each circuit once, and the report of circuit costs takes `by` of `group`, and a group in its filter, so that the cost of a group is read without picking its circuits out site by site. A circuit's record lists its groups, and the list of circuits finds them by `groups.name`. The routes are `/circuit-groups`, `POST /circuit-groups/{hid}/retire`, `GET` and `POST /circuit-groups/{hid}/members`, and `DELETE /circuit-groups/{hid}/members/{member_hid}`. Scenario S48 reads: give the circuit three monthly cost lines; enter a second circuit of the same carrier; put both in one group; the monthly cost of each is the sum of its lines and its annual cost twelve times that, and the report totals the annual cost of both by carrier, by site, each at its A side, and by the group, with the monthly figure beside it. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-15 that circuits be added to groups, a circuit to more than one, to see a collection that lands at different sites, and the cost of its circuits as one sum. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-152.) |
| D-220 | **Circuits in files.** Seven entities: `vendor_contacts`, `circuit_groups`, `circuits`, `circuit_carriers`, `circuit_costs`, `circuit_contacts` and `circuit_devices`. A row of `circuits` finds its circuit by `hid`; then by `enterprise_circuit_id`; then by `carrier` with `carrier_circuit_number`, the number that carrier gives the circuit, as its primary or as another; then by a `name` that one circuit alone has among those that are not retired; and is otherwise a new circuit. More than one answer is an error on that row, never a guess. A column that names a circuit, `circuit` in the other files, takes its HID, its enterprise circuit ID or a name that one circuit alone has, and an export writes the enterprise circuit ID where there is one, then the name, and the HID where another circuit answers to the same name. The columns of `circuits` are `name`, `enterprise_circuit_id`, `description`, `circuit_type`, `port_mbps`, `down_mbps`, `up_mbps`, `a_location`, `a_note`, `z_location`, `z_note`, `cost_side`, `carrier`, `carrier_circuit_number`, `billing_account`, `billing_contract`, `installed_on`, `activated_on`, `disconnected_on`, `production`, `support_weight`, `owner`, `groups`, `notes`, `record_status` and `tags`; an export also writes `speed`, `monthly_cost`, `annual_cost`, `a_site`, `z_site`, `cost_site` and `support_weight_source`. A location is named by its path or its HID, as on an item; `groups` names the circuit's groups by their names, joined with `;`, and is the whole list, as `tags` is. A carrier that is no vendor yet is made one, `unreviewed`, as for every file but one of purchase lines (P-14, D-186). A new circuit with no `cost_side` takes the setting's. A row of `circuit_carriers` names `circuit`, `vendor`, `role`, `circuit_number`, `priority` and `notes`, and is found by its circuit and its vendor; of `circuit_costs`, `circuit`, `description`, `monthly_cost`, `vendor`, `starts_on`, `ends_on` and `notes`, found by its circuit and its description; of `circuit_devices`, `circuit`, `side` and one of `item` and `group`, found by its circuit and its device; of `circuit_contacts`, `circuit`, `side`, blank for one of the carrier's, `role`, `contact` and `priority`, found by its circuit, side, role and contact; of `vendor_contacts`, `vendor`, `role`, `contact` and `priority`, found by its vendor, role and contact; of `circuit_groups`, `name`, `description`, `owner`, `notes`, `record_status` and `tags`, found by its name. An import removes no carrier, cost line, device or contact of a circuit. In the load order the contacts of vendors follow the contacts, and the circuits come last: circuit groups, circuits, their carriers, their cost lines, their contacts, and their devices, which follow the items and the device groups. `examples/` gain fictional circuits of each kind. Scenario S50 reads as the build plan has it. After the review of the server: `carrier` and `carrier_circuit_number` are the primary carrier's columns, and find a circuit by the primary carrier's number only; and two circuits of one name with neither an enterprise circuit ID nor a primary carrier's number cannot be told apart by a file loaded without their HIDs, as two items of one name with neither a serial number nor an asset tag cannot, so such circuits are given an enterprise circuit ID to travel. Why: The build plan says that circuits load from files and that a row finds its circuit by the enterprise circuit ID; this says what else finds one, which columns there are, and where they come in the load order. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-153.) |
| D-221 | **Circuits in lists, at the command line and in the web UI.** `GET /circuits` lists, filters, sorts, pages and exports as the item list does, by every field of the record, by `carrier.name`, by `carriers.vendor_hid` and `carriers.circuit_number`, any carrier of the circuit, by `location`, a side at a location or `within` the path of a location, a site or a region, by `cost_site_hid`, `groups.name`, `owner.label`, `owner_exception`, `effective.support_weight`, `effective.down_mbps`, `effective.up_mbps`, `monthly_cost`, `annual_cost` and `in_service`, today, and by `tags`, and leaves out drafts and retired circuits unless asked; `/circuit-groups` lists likewise. The command line has `hirdir circuits list`, `show`, by the HID, the enterprise circuit ID or a name one circuit alone has, `set`, `retire`, `add-contact` and `remove-contact`; `hirdir circuit-groups list`, `show`, `add` and `remove`; `hirdir vendors add-contact` and `remove-contact`; and `hirdir report circuit-costs`; files go through `hirdir import` and `hirdir export` as for every entity. The web UI has a Circuits page: the grid of circuits, with Name, Enterprise ID, Type, Speed, Carrier, Carrier's number, A side, Z side, Monthly cost, Annual cost, Owner and Tags shown at first and the rest in the column chooser; and a page for each circuit, with its fields, each side with its location's path and its site contacts with the level each came from, its carriers with their numbers, the carrier's account team and support desk with where they came from, the devices on each side, its cost lines with the monthly and annual totals, and its groups, and the forms to change and retire it. An item's page and a device group's list the circuits they are connected to, a location's page the circuits that have a side there, and a vendor's page its contacts and its circuits. Circuit groups have a list and a page each, with their circuits and their cost. The report of circuit costs is a tab of Reports, with the year and the grouping to choose, each row leading to its circuits. Why: The build plan says that circuits are listed, filtered, sorted and exported as items are, in the API, the command line, files and the web UI. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-154.) |
| D-222 | **The particulars of the support weight of a place (P-139), and of a default and a minimum (P-141).** A region, a site and a location have `support_weight`, a decimal of two places, not below zero, blank unless given, set by hand, by a file of places and by the API, and audited; it is not inherited by the places beneath. Six settings of the inventory, each blank unless an Admin sets it, a number of two places from 0 to 1,000,000: `default_site_weight`, `minimum_site_weight`, `default_item_weight`, `minimum_item_weight`, `default_circuit_weight` and `minimum_circuit_weight`. A setting with no default is new, and `GET /settings` lists it with a `default` of null. The weight in force of a **major item** is its own, its model's, then `default_item_weight`, with the source `own`, `catalog`, `setting` or `none`; of a **site**, its own, then `default_site_weight`; of a **circuit**, its own, then `default_circuit_weight`; and of a region and a location, its own alone. Where a minimum of its kind is set, a weight in force below it is raised to it, and `effective.support_weight` then says `raised_from`, the weight before it was raised; nothing, a blank with no default, is not raised. A minor item has its weight as now, and neither a default nor a minimum, since a minor item carries no weight. An item's weight is resolved and raised for one unit, then multiplied by its quantity, and the stack discount is applied after that, so that a stack and a switch alone start from the same weight. The default and the minimum apply wherever an item's weight in force is used: the summary, the scoreboard, the portfolio of a vendor and the capacity report. **Demand.** The support weight of the capacity report is that of the items, as now, and of the places and the circuits beside them: the weight in force of each region, site and location that is active, or a draft with the drafts, and of each circuit that is active, or a draft with the drafts, and not disconnected before today. The report answers `support_weight` whole and its parts, `items_weight`, `places_weight` and `circuits_weight`, and how many items, sites and circuits counted at a default, `defaulted`, and how many were raised to a minimum, `raised`. `demand_by_region` counts a place's own weight in the row of each region at or above it, and a circuit's in that of each region at or above the site it counts at (P-150), so that the regions at the top and what is nowhere still add up to the whole. Why: The owner asked for P-139 and answered P-141 on 2026-11-15; this names the fields and the settings, and how the capacity report counts them. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-155.) |
| D-223 | **The particulars of Try, the what-if of the capacity report (P-141).** `POST /reports/capacity/try` answers the capacity report as it is, `in_force`, and as it would be, `tried`, beside what was tried: any of the six settings of P-155, a key left out being as it is and `null` trying it blank; `weight_per_resource_unit`, the support weight one engineer carries; `stack_discount_pct`, the global stack discount, which a model that sets its own still overrides; and `staff`, the staff lines to try: `add`, lines that are not there, each with a `name`, a `quantity` and `units_each`; `change`, lines that are there, by HID, with another `quantity` or `units_each`; and `leave_out`, lines that are there, by HID, counted as if they were not. A staff line that is tried adds, and is active and in date in both `active` and `with_drafts`; a line that multiplies works on a changed line as it is tried, on none that is left out, and on no line that is added, since it names the lines it works on. Nothing is saved, nothing is written to the audit trail, and the answer is never kept; any role may Try, as any role reads the report. An Admin who has agreed an estimate saves the settings by `PUT /settings/{key}`, one audited change each, and a staff line is saved by entering it. A value that the setting would refuse is a `422` that names it. The Capacity page of the web UI has a Try panel: the settings and figures in force to change, the staff lines with their quantities to change and a box to leave each out, rows to add, and the two balances side by side; an Admin is offered to save the settings tried. Why: The owner answered on 2026-11-15 that Try changes the defaults and minimums, the support weight one engineer carries and the stack discount, and adds, changes or leaves out staff without saving them as drafts. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-156.) |
| D-224 | **The particulars of the long reports worked out ahead, and of the check of what is kept (P-142).** The capacity report, `GET /reports/capacity`, is kept as the coverage report is (D-184), with the same stamp, its `demand_by_region` with it; a Try is never kept. Just after midnight in the application's time zone, and when the server starts, the server works out and keeps the coverage report, the renewal worklist, the summary of the renewal alert and the capacity report, each for the view its page opens with: the whole inventory, today, with no filter, and the counts the page asks for first. `GET /kept-answers`, for an Admin, lists what is kept: the report, the request whole, when it was worked out and how many times it has been given since. `POST /kept-answers/check`, for an Admin, works out each kept answer anew from the records, compares it with what was kept, keeps the new one, and answers `checked_at`, how many were checked, and `differed`: for each answer that was not the same, the report and the request. None should differ, and one that did is a defect to report. The check is an audit event, `kept_answers_checked`, with what it found; it changes no record and leaves the kept answers good, as a sign-in does. The Settings page of the web UI has a button for an Admin, Check kept reports, which says how many were checked and names any that differed. Why: P-142 left the route of the check and the shape of its answer to be settled when 0.8.0 was started. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.8.0. (Was P-157.) |
| D-225 | **A reference, and the records that take one (D-157).** A reference is a record of its own, `reference_files`, referenced by HID, audited and role-checked as the inventory is: a file that is uploaded and kept by `hirdir`, or a link to one that is kept elsewhere, an address as a link of D-189 is. Each has a `title`, required, at most 200 characters, with neither `<` nor `>`; a `kind`, required, a value of a new editable list, `reference_kind`, loaded by 0.9.0 with `photograph`, `map`, `sketch`, `floor_plan`, `diagram`, `administration_guide`, `installation_guide`, `data_sheet`, `release_notes`, `letter_of_authorization`, `test_results` and `other`; and `notes`. A file has `file_name`, the name it was uploaded with, whose extension decides its type, and `media_type`, `size_bytes` and `sha256`, the checksum of its content, which are worked out from it; a link has `url`. A reference is attached to one record or more, `reference_attachments`, each naming exactly one catalog entry, region, site, location, circuit or item, and a record has a reference once: an administration guide is uploaded once and attached to every model it covers, and a new edition of it is uploaded once. A record lists, each time it is read, the references attached to it and those it has from the records it stands under: a place its own, then those of the places above it, nearest first: the parent locations, the site, and each region outward; an item its own, then those of its model, then those of the location it is at in force and of the places above that; a catalog entry and a circuit their own. Each is listed once, at the nearest level that has it, with `source`, one of `own`, `catalog`, `location`, `site` and `region`, and the record it came from, and nothing is copied down: a floor plan attached to a floor is listed in every room and rack on it, and taking it off the floor takes it from all of them. A retired record keeps its references and takes new ones, as a closed site keeps its papers. Why: The owner answered on 2026-11-16 that regions, sites, locations, catalog entries, circuits and single devices take files, that a guide is uploaded once and attached to many, and that a place lists its own files and then those above it, each marked with where it is attached. That an item lists those of its location as well as those of its model, and that a circuit lists its own alone, are my reading: a technician at a device looks for the floor plan of its room, and a circuit's two sides already lead to their places. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-158.) |
| D-226 | **The kinds of file, and their size.** A file may be up to 100 MB: `max_reference_megabytes` under `[files]` in the configuration file, from 1 to 2,048, with nginx allowing as much; an import stays at 20 MB, `max_megabytes`, and so does the body of every other request, which the application refuses itself above that. The kinds of file taken are a setting of the inventory, `reference_file_types`, the list of extensions an Admin may change, loaded with the pictures `jpg`, `jpeg`, `png`, `gif`, `webp`, `heic`, `heif`, `tif` and `tiff`; `pdf`; the files of Office and Visio, `doc`, `docx`, `xls`, `xlsx`, `ppt`, `pptx`, `vsd` and `vsdx`; the drawings `dwg` and `dxf`; plain text, `txt`, `csv` and `log`; and `zip`, for a vendor's documentation set. An Admin may add any other extension of letters and digits, up to ten, whose files are then kept and downloaded as `application/octet-stream`, but never one of a web page, a script or a program, which is fixed and listed as `refused_file_types`: `htm`, `html`, `shtml`, `xhtml`, `mht`, `mhtml`, `svg`, `svgz`, `xml`, `xsl`, `xslt`, `js`, `mjs`, `cjs`, `wasm`, `swf`, `exe`, `msi`, `msp`, `dll`, `com`, `scr`, `pif`, `bat`, `cmd`, `ps1`, `psm1`, `vbs`, `vbe`, `wsf`, `wsh`, `hta`, `jar`, `apk`, `app`, `dmg`, `deb`, `rpm`, `sh`, `php`, `py`, `pl`, `rb`, `lnk`, `reg`, `msc` and `cpl`. Where hirdir knows an extension the content must be what it says: a PDF begins as a PDF does, a picture as its format does, a file of Office or Visio of today and a ZIP as a ZIP archive, one of the Office of before 2007 as such a file, and a text file holds no zero byte; and no file is taken, whatever its name, whose content begins as a program or a web page does; the checks read the first 512 bytes. A file over the limit is refused with `413`, and one of a kind that is not taken, or whose content is not what its name says, with `422` and the reason. A picture that a browser shows, JPEG, PNG, GIF and WebP, is shown in the page; every other file is downloaded, and none is ever opened within `hirdir`. The content is served with `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`, and is not compressed again, but for text, which is compressed on the way as every answer of more than a kilobyte is; the page's own policy allows it to show a picture that it has fetched itself, `img-src 'self' blob:`, and nothing more. Why: The owner answered 100 MB, and the list, which an Admin may change, with pictures shown and the rest downloaded, and web pages, SVG, scripts and programs refused. The check of the content is mine, so that a program given another name is refused rather than kept. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-159.) |
| D-227 | **Taking a reference off, erasing it, and a new edition.** Taking a reference off a record removes the attachment outright, with an audit event that holds what it was. A reference taken off the last record it is on is erased with it, which the answer says, and `DELETE /reference-files/{hid}` erases one from every record at once: the record and its content are gone, and the audit event keeps its title, kind, file name, size, checksum and the records it was on. A new edition, `PUT /reference-files/{hid}/content`, replaces the content of a file and keeps the reference, its title, its kind and its attachments, and the content it had is erased; a link has no content and is changed by its `url`. Content that another reference holds too is kept for that one. The backups on the host keep a copy of what was erased until they are rotated out, 14 days unless `[backup] keep` says otherwise, so that every backup restores whole: the page says so before it erases, and a copy moved off the host is beyond `hirdir`'s reach. A file whose content is on a record already, under any reference, is refused there with `409` that names that reference, so that an upload sent a second time makes no second record; an upload therefore takes no `Idempotency-Key`, and refuses one with a `422`. Why: The owner answered on 2026-11-16 that removing a file deletes its content at once, and, asked again, that the backups on the host keep their copy until they rotate out. That a file is erased when it comes off the last record it is on, and that a new edition erases the content before it, are my reading of "at once" for one file on many records. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-160.) |
| D-228 | **Where the content is kept, and the backup.** The content of each file is kept once, whatever the number of references that hold it, in `files/` under the state directory, `/var/lib/hirdir/files/`, in a file named for its SHA-256 checksum, and never in the database: a preview of an import works on a copy of the database, and the daily backup copies it whole, which neither should do with gigabytes of photographs. Content is written before its record and removed after the last record that names it is gone, under one lock of the server process, so that what the database names is there. `hirdir admin backup` copies the database as now and puts into `backups/files/` the content its copy names that is not there yet, as a hard link where the two directories are on one file system and as a copy where they are not, so that each file is held once among all the backups; the rotation that keeps the newest 14 removes from there what no backup that is kept names. `hirdir admin restore FILE` takes the content from the `files/` beside the backup, or from `--files DIR`, checks before it changes anything that it has the content of every file the backup names, each with its checksum, and refuses, naming how many and the first of them, where one is missing; with `--allow-missing-files` it restores all the same and lists them, and each answers a download with a `404` that says so. Content that the restored database does not name is set aside in `files.before-restore/`, beside `hirdir.db.before-restore`. To restore on another host, the backup is copied with the `files/` beside it. `hirdir admin reset` erases every reference with its content, after the backup it takes. `hirdir admin check` counts the files and what they take, and names content that is missing and content that no record names. An upload cut short leaves a part in `files/.incoming/`, which is removed when the server starts, with any content that no record names and that is more than a day old. An upgrade leaves the files as they were. Why: The build plan asks for the files in the backup, back with a restore, and left as they were by an upgrade. Keeping each content once among the backups, rather than once in each of the 14, is mine. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-161.) |
| D-229 | **The routes, the roles, the command line and the page.** `GET /reference-files` lists every reference, paged as the other tables are, sorted by `title`, `kind`, `form`, `file_name`, `size_bytes`, `attachment_count`, `created_at` and `updated_at`, and narrowed by `kind`, `form`, `file` or `link`, and `title`, which the title contains. `POST /reference-files` uploads one: the file is the body of the request, as an import's is (D-80), with `title`, `kind`, `file_name` and `attach_to`, the HIDs of the records it goes on, from one to 100, in the address, which a proxy holds to a few kilobytes; more records are given it with its attachments, and its notes with a `PATCH`. `POST /reference-files/links` makes a link from `title`, `kind`, `url`, `notes` and `attach_to`. `GET /reference-files/{hid}` answers one with the records it is on; `PATCH /reference-files/{hid}` changes its `title`, `kind` and `notes`, and the `url` of a link; `PUT /reference-files/{hid}/content` takes a new edition, with `file_name` and `row_version` in the address; `GET /reference-files/{hid}/content` downloads it; `DELETE /reference-files/{hid}` erases it. `POST /reference-files/{hid}/attachments` puts it on more records, `records`, up to 1,000 at a time, and `DELETE /reference-files/{hid}/attachments/{attachment_hid}` takes it off one. What a record has is `GET .../{hid}/references` under `/catalog-entries`, `/regions`, `/sites`, `/locations`, `/circuits` and `/items`, paged, its own first and then each level outward. Any role reads, lists and downloads; the Inventory role uploads, links, changes, puts on, takes off, replaces and erases; an Admin changes the kinds of file taken. Every upload, link, change, new edition, attachment, removal of one, erasure and download is an audit event; a download, `download`, changes nothing and leaves the kept answers good. nginx lets through the larger of the two limits, which `hirdir-setup --renew` writes into the snippet from the configuration file, so that a site that changes the limit changes it in one place. The command line has `hirdir references`: `list`, `show`, `add` of one file or several, `link`, `get`, `set`, `replace`, `attach`, `detach` and `remove`, a record named as `location:PATH`, `site:PATH`, `region:PATH`, `model:VENDOR PID`, `item:NAME`, `circuit:ID` or by its HID. The web UI gives the page of each of the six records a section, Files and links, and the Admin area a page, Files, of every reference. Why: The build plan lists upload, link, download, limits, roles and audit; the routes, the commands and the pages are mine. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-162.) |
| D-230 | **The links of 0.7.0 stay as they are.** A link of D-189 stays a field of its record, an address and its text, carried in the file of that record, and is listed first in the section Files and links, as the record's own. A reference that is a link is a record of its own, with a title and a kind, that may be on several records and on an item or a circuit, which have no links. Folding the links of 0.7.0 into the references, with the files that carry them, is left until it is asked for. Why: The build plan lists files and links together, and S46 holds what a file does with a link. Changing what the files of places and of the catalog carry is a larger change than 0.9.0 needs. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-163.) |
| D-231 | **What the lanes of 0.9.0 chose where the pages were silent.** A restore puts the content it needs into the store first, linked from the backups' own `files/` as a backup links it and copied from anywhere else; then it sets aside what the restored database does not name in `files.before-restore/`; and it puts the database in place last, in one step, so that a restore stopped at any point leaves every content in the store or set aside. It refuses to restore `hirdir.db.before-restore`, or the database itself, which it writes over before it reads, and says to copy it elsewhere first. One backup or restore is at work at a time, under a lock in the backup directory, and a second waits. A content that cannot be read is counted with what is missing, and `hirdir admin check` looks again at a content that seems missing or unnamed before it reports it. A kept backup that cannot be read stops the pruning of `backups/files/`, with a warning, rather than remove what it may name. `hirdir-setup --renew` leaves the snippet as it is where the configuration file cannot be read, and the install or upgrade goes on and says so; a limit lowered by hand in a snippet of 0.8.0 is raised at the upgrade to the larger limit of the configuration file, as install.md says. Going back to 0.8.0 keeps the snippet of 0.9.0, which the setup tool of 0.8.0 does not know and leaves alone with a message: nginx then lets 100 MB through, and 0.8.0 refuses more than 20 MB itself, which the owner accepted on 2026-11-16. The backup of the files stays in `cli/admin.py`, beside the backup as it was. `files.before-restore/` grew with each restore until somebody removed it; the owner asked on 2026-11-16 that it be tidied, which P-165 does in 0.9.1. In the web UI: a retired record takes references from the Inventory role, while its links of 0.7.0 stay as they were; the page puts an upload on its own record and others by Put on other records; Admin › Files takes nothing off, which is done on the record's page; an item shows the links of its model where it showed them. After the review of the server: an XML file that is no web page is taken as text, a text file is compressed on the way as any answer is, and one upload goes on at most 100 records, the rest by its attachments. Why: The lanes and the review met each of these where the documentation said nothing or could not be kept as written; each is the safer or the plainer reading, and easy to change. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.0. (Was P-164.) |
| D-232 | **What a restore keeps set aside, tidied.** `files.before-restore/` holds what undoing the last restore takes: the content that `hirdir.db.before-restore`, the copy of the database as it was before that restore, names and the restored database does not. Each restore, once it has set aside what the restored database does not name and before it puts the database in place, lets go of every other content there, which an earlier restore set aside for a copy of the database that is gone since and which no database that remains names, with any part that a move cut short, and says how many. Undoing the last restore, as install.md shows, finds every file it needs in the store or there. A site that wants to keep what a restore set aside beyond the next one copies the two elsewhere first. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16, answering 0.9.0, that `files.before-restore/` be tidied rather than grow with each restore, and that it come in 0.9.1, 0.9.0 being published as it was tested. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.1. (Was P-165.) |
| D-233 | **The examples loaded by one command.** `hirdir admin load-examples` loads the fictional inventory of `examples/` into an empty inventory, so that a site can try `hirdir` and then clear it with `hirdir admin reset`, which removes what was loaded and whatever was added since, and nothing of the site's own. It is a command on the server and nowhere else, as reset is (D-74): no route and no page. It refuses while the service is running, as reset does: the worker of the server would take the imports of the examples, which no account asked for, and stop them. It refuses where the inventory holds any record that a reset clears, a reference among them, so that the examples are never mixed with a site's own records, and where an import is queued or being applied, which would be applied beside them; a stored preview, a renewal alert that was seen, a key of an idempotent request and the weekly record of the capacity balance do not count, and `domain/reset.py` names them. It works each CSV file in the load order (D-79, D-123, D-153, P-153) as an import does, a preview and then an apply, through the same functions, so that no rule is written twice: each is a stored import named for its file, made and applied by nobody, audited as `system`, and worked with the Inventory role, which is what an import asks of anybody. A file with a rejected row, as a choice that the site has removed can make, stops the load there: the command names the file and its first rejected rows with their reasons, and says that what the files before it made stays, which a reset clears. It prints how many records each file made, and ends by saying how to clear them. Accounts and settings are not touched. The application carries its own copy of the files, inside its Python package under `/opt/hirdir`, so that a host whose dpkg leaves out `/usr/share/doc`, as a minimal or container image does, still has them, and the package build checks that the copy is `examples/`; a development checkout loads `examples/` itself. The copy in `/usr/share/doc/hirdir/examples/` stays, to read and to import by hand. The dates of the contracts are fixed in the files, so what the coverage report says of the examples changes as the calendar moves on, as install.md says. It adds no scenario, as a release of corrections adds none: S12 is worked by it too, the counts matching the seed, a second run refusing, and a reset then leaving the inventory empty. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16 for a command that loads the examples, after asking whether a reset removes them, and that it come in 0.9.1. Refusing an inventory that holds records is what makes a reset take the examples and nothing of the site's. Stopping the service first, the stored imports, the Inventory role, stopping at a rejected row and the copy inside the package are mine. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.1. (Was P-166.) |
| D-234 | **The menu bar stays in view when a page opens.** A page opened from the menu bar, or from a link on another page, begins at its top, with the menu bar in view. Focus moves to the content of the page, as it has since 0.1.0, so that a keyboard or a screen reader starts reading there, but the content is not scrolled into view: until 0.9.1 the browser scrolled the content of every page taller than its window to the top of the window, which pushed the menu bar out of sight by as much as the menu bar and the renewal alert take, on Groups, Addresses, Vendors, Owners and most other pages, though not on a page still loading as it opened, as Items often is. Back and Forward leave the window where the browser puts it, and a change that keeps the page's address, such as its filter, does not move it. Why: The owner found the menu bar scrolled out of view on opening some pages, after 0.9.1. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.2. (Was P-167.) |
| D-235 | **The name of the product, as a site calls it.** A setting of the inventory, `product_name`, `hirdir` unless an Admin gives another, such as `Example Inventory`: 1 to 40 characters once the spaces at either end are taken off, with neither `<`, `>`, a control character nor one that turns the direction of the text, the joiners that some scripts and emoji need being taken; `null` returns it to `hirdir`. It is shown wherever the web page names the product: at the upper left of the menu bar, in the title of the browser's tab, `Items · Example Inventory`, on the sign-in page, `Sign in to Example Inventory`, and in what the pages say, as "ask an administrator of Example Inventory". About gives it as the product, and beside it that the software is hirdir, with its version. The names of downloaded files begin with it: an export, `Example-Inventory-items-20261116T091500-0500.csv`, and the scoreboard's two files, `Example-Inventory-scoreboard-sites.csv`. In a file name letters first lose their accents, `Réseau` being `Reseau`, then each run of characters other than ASCII letters and digits, `.`, `_` and `-` is one `-`, a `-` or a `.` at either end is taken off, and `hirdir` stands where nothing is left; an import still guesses the kind of record of a file so named. What keeps the name hirdir: the commands, `hirdir`, `hirdir admin` and `hirdir-setup`, the package, the service and its paths on the host, the names of the backups, `name` in `GET /about`, the title of the OpenAPI document, the `schema` of a JSON file, `hirdir/1`, the header `X-Hirdir-Client`, the documentation, and the name of a reference file, which is its own. The sign-in page shows the name before anybody has signed in: the server writes it into the page it serves, by whatever path it is asked for, as it writes the prefix (P-05), so that no route is open without signing in beyond those of D-08; where the database cannot be read, the page is served all the same, named hirdir. A change shows on the Admin's own page at once, and on another person's when the page is next loaded. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16, after 0.9.1, for a name of the enterprise's choosing at the upper left of the menu bar, and answered that an Admin sets it in Admin › Settings, audited, and that it replaces hirdir in all of the web page and in the names of downloaded files. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.2. (Was P-168.) |
| D-236 | **Whom to ask: the team that runs this installation.** Four settings of the inventory, each blank unless an Admin sets one: `help_organization`, the name of the group that runs it, at most 200 characters; `help_email`, a plain email address, at most 254 characters, with one `@`, something before it, a domain with a dot after it, no space and none of `? & = % / : ; , " '`, which would add a copy, a subject or a body to the message that a link to it opens; `help_phone`, at most 40 characters, of the digits 0 to 9, spaces, letters and `+ - ( ) . , # *`, with a digit among them, shown as it is typed, and called by a link that dials its `+` and its digits up to the first letter, comma or `#`, where an extension begins; and `help_url`, an address that begins with `https://` or `http://`, at most 2,000 characters with no space, as a link of D-189 is. None of them holds `<`, `>`, a control character or one that turns the direction of the text, and `null` makes one blank again. A fifth, `help_on_sign_in`, false unless an Admin makes it true, lists them on the sign-in page as well. About lists them under "Whom to ask": the organization, the email address to write to, the number to call and the address to open in a new tab, each that is set and none that is not; where none is set, About says that nobody has been named, and tells an Admin that they are set in Admin › Settings. `GET /about`, which every role reads once signed in, answers `product_name` and `help`, with `organization`, `email`, `phone` and `url`, each `null` where it is blank. The sign-in page, where `help_on_sign_in` is true and one of them is set, says under the form that someone who cannot sign in should ask them, and lists them as About does. The server writes them into the page it serves only then: while it is false, nothing of them is in a page that is read before signing in, as the sign-in page is open to whoever reaches the site. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16, after 0.9.1, that a user be able to see who runs the tool and whom to ask: the organization's name, an email address, a phone number and an address on the web, on About. They answered that an Admin sets them in Admin › Settings, and that the sign-in page lists them only where an Admin chooses, being seen by anyone who reaches the site. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.2. (Was P-169.) |
| D-237 | **The kinds of external system, an editable list.** `external_systems.kind` is a value of a new editable list, `system_kind`, in place of the fixed list of D-146, so that a site names, renames, adds and retires the kinds of the tools it registers its gear with, as it does the kinds of location and of reference. 0.9.3 loads the list with the eight kinds that were fixed, in their order and with the labels the web page has shown since 0.6.0: `monitoring`, Monitoring; `config_backup`, Configuration backup; `access_control`, Access control; `sdn_controller`, SDN controller; `cloud_service`, Cloud service; `dns`, DNS; `discovery`, Discovery; and `other`, Other. A value that a site has put into the list by other means is left as it is, and every system keeps the kind it has. The list is edited as every list is (D-60): on Admin › Choices, by `/choices` and from the command line, by the Inventory and the Admin roles, and audited. A label is changed, which is what people read, and the value stored on each system stays. A value is added. One that no system holds is removed; one that a system holds is only made inactive, which offers it no more while the systems that hold it keep it, through any change that leaves the kind alone. A system that is made with no kind, by hand, by the API or by a file, is given `other` while `other` is an active value of the list. Where it is not, because it has been made inactive or removed, a system with no kind is refused, with a reason that names the field, and is never given another value in its place. A kind that the list does not offer is refused as it is now, with the values that it does offer. The page offers the active values with their labels, and the list of systems and the page of a system show the label. The contract gives `kind` as text rather than as the eight values, and a client reads the values from `GET /choices?list=system_kind`. Nothing in `hirdir` treats one kind apart from the others: no rule and no report reads it, and it says what a system is. The list is loaded by a migration, so going back to 0.9.2 is by the backup the upgrade took, as from every release that changes the schema; 0.9.2 refuses a kind of the site's own. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16, after 0.9.2 was published, why the kinds of external system were not among the choices that may be changed, and then that they be made so in 0.9.3. The fixed list came from the first design and was carried into D-146 with no reason of its own, while the lists of 0.8.0 and 0.9.0 that say what kind a record is were made editable, and D-177 holds that what a site could reasonably want otherwise is not held in the code. The owner agreed that a system with no kind is refused where `other` is not offered. Loading the list by a migration, keeping a held value through a change that leaves it alone, and the labels as the page shows them are mine. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.3. (Was P-170.) |
| D-238 | **A setting is changed where it is listed.** On Admin › Settings, Change opens the form of that setting directly beneath its row, across the width of the table, and not below the last of the settings: the setting, its value and its meaning stay in view above the form, and once it is set or cancelled the page is where it was. Focus moves into the form when it opens, and back to that setting's Change button when it closes. One form is open at a time: Change on another setting closes the first, and what was typed in it and not set is let go. What the form holds is as before: the setting's hint, the values to choose from where it takes a list, Set, Back to the default, Cancel, and what was refused. The same holds wherever a form changes one row of a table: Edit on Admin › Tags and Admin › Lifecycle rules, Change on Admin › Files, and a change in the Files and links section of a record's page each open the form beneath the row they change. The Settings table fits a window 1280 pixels wide with nothing scrolled: a long name of a setting wraps, and the two buttons of a row stand one above the other in their cell; a narrower window scrolls the table sideways as before. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16, after 0.9.2 was published: the form opened below every setting, so an Admin clicked Change, scrolled down, set it and scrolled back up. They offered either a change in the row or a form under the row. The form under the row is mine: a list of states or of file kinds needs the room that one cell does not give. The web lane found the same form below the table on Tags, Lifecycle rules, Files and the Files and links of a record, and the Settings table 1,453 pixels wide in a column of 1,140 at a window of 1,280, its Change buttons half out of view; asked, the owner chose the same change on every one of those pages and a table that fits. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.3. (Was P-171.) |
| D-239 | **People are contacts, and a user and an operator are each a part one of them plays.** `contacts` is the one list of people: each person, or shared mailbox, once, with what is true of them whatever part they play: `name`, `organization`, `email`, `phone`, a second number `phone2`, `notes`, `record_status` and tags, each but the name optional. A part that a person plays is a record of its own that names the contact, `contact_hid`, and holds only what belongs to that part. Nothing is copied from the contact to the part: the part reads the contact each time it is read, so that a person who signs in, looks after a set of devices under a label and is the technical contact of a site is one contact, and a new number entered once shows in all three places. There are two such parts, and one that comes later is added in the same way. **A user** is the part of a person who signs in: `username`, the access code, `is_service_account`, `disabled`, its groups and its roles, and `contact_hid`, the contact it is. `display_name` and `email` are no longer kept on the user. A user answers `contact`, that contact's `hid`, `name`, `email`, `phone`, `phone2` and `record_status`, and, to read only, `display_name` and `email`, which are the contact's name and email, blank where it has no contact. A contact is the contact of one user at most. A service account is no person and has no contact: a contact given to one is refused. A user is made with the contact it is, `contact_hid`; or with `contact`, the `name`, `email`, `organization`, `phone` and `phone2` of a new contact, made in the same change and audited as a contact made by hand; or with neither, and is then shown by its username until an Admin gives it one. An Admin gives a user its contact and takes it away by `contact_hid` on `PATCH /users/{hid}`; a contact that is retired, or is another user's, is refused. The email and the numbers that a user shows are its contact's, changed by whoever may change a contact, the Inventory and the Admin roles, and a user's page changes them on its contact. A contact that is a user's is retired as any other is: the user still signs in, and its page shows the contact as retired. Disabling a user changes nothing of its contact. A contact answers `is_user`, true where it is a user's, which filters and sorts the list of contacts, and `user`, the `hid` and `username` of that user, both read by every role: whoever signs in is of the team that runs the network, who read the enterprise's directory of people already. **An operator** is the part of a person who looks after what the label is on, as it has been: `label`, `description`, `colour`, `contact_hid` and `record_status`. The label is handed over by giving it another contact, and is open where it has none or one that is retired, as before. An operator is **internal** where its contact is a user's, and **external** where its contact is not a user's, external to `hirdir`: `scope`, `internal` or `external`, to read only, blank where nobody is behind the label, as where its contact is retired and it is open. The record of an operator answers its contact with that contact's `email`, `phone` and `phone2`, and `user`, the user that contact is, where it is one; `scope` filters and sorts the list of operators, and `operator.scope` the list of items. The page of an operator offers, for the person behind it, the contacts that are users, the other contacts, and a new person, whose name, email and two numbers make a new contact. The email and the numbers are shown with the operator, and changed there on its contact, which changes them wherever that person is named. **In files** a contact has `phone2`, and an export of contacts has `user`, the username, to read only. An export of operators has `scope`, `contact_email`, `contact_phone` and `contact_phone2`, to read only. Users are not carried by files, as before (D-79). **The upgrade** adds `phone2` to contacts and `contact_hid` to users, and gives each user that is no service account and has a display name or an email a contact: the contact that has its email already, where one has, which is left as it is; or a new one, named with its display name, or with its username where it has none, and holding its email. A second user with the email of a contact that is already another user's is given none, as a contact is one user's at most. It writes an audit event, made by `system`, for each contact it makes and each user it gives one. It then drops `display_name` and `email` from users, so a service account is shown by its username alone and loses the display name it had. Going back to 0.9.2 is by the backup the upgrade took. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16, after 0.9.2 was published, that an operator may be a user or a person who is not one, that an operator have an email and two numbers, which are a user's where the operator is a user, that a user have two numbers beside its email, and that the operators linked to users be called internal and the others external, external to hirdir. Asked where an outside person's details are kept, they chose a contact, with a second number. They then asked whether users should be contacts too, chose that every person be a contact, and described the model: Contacts the common list of people with their email, numbers and other common attributes, and User and Operator each a class of people that adds the few elements of its own role. Asked whether the upgrade makes the contacts of existing users, they answered that a breaking change is acceptable before 1.0.0. Asked, after the review, whether every role reads the username of the user a contact is, they first chose an Admin alone, and then, when the second review found the audit trail telling it to every role, reverted that: hirdir is used by trusted members of the IT operations team on an internal network, any of whom can read the enterprise's directory of people, and nothing is to be hidden from a signed-in role that is not hidden from them there. Making them at the upgrade, with the contact that has the user's email taken where there is one, the audit events of the upgrade, a user with no contact allowed, `scope` blank where nobody is behind a label, and the username readable with a contact are mine. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.3. (Was P-172.) |
| D-240 | **A username is changed.** An Admin gives a user another username, `username` on `PATCH /users/{hid}`, under the rule that a new one meets: 1 to 64 letters, digits, dots, hyphens and underscores, unique without regard to case, a change of its case alone being allowed. It is one audited change of the user, with the old name and the new. The user is the same record, by its HID: its contact, its groups, its roles, its sessions and its service tokens are kept, and the person signs in under the new name from then on, the old name signing nobody in, as any name that is no user's. The audit trail, the history of a record and the stored imports name whoever made a change by the username they have when it is read, so the new name stands in the events before the rename as well, and the event of the rename keeps both. An Admin renames themselves as any user. The Users page and a user's page offer it to an Admin, and the form that makes a user says that the username is what the person signs in with and that an Admin can change it later. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-16 whether a user can be renamed, the display name being changeable and the username not, and preferred that a rename be allowed to a warning when a user is made. Keeping the sessions, and naming a user in the trail by the name they have now, are mine: the trail holds the user's HID, and a rename is no sign-in. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.3. (Was P-173.) |
| D-241 | **What the review of the server of 0.9.3 found, and how it is put right.** A reset, `hirdir admin reset`, keeps the contact each user is, with its history, as it keeps the accounts: until now it could not run once a user had a contact, which the contact's key in `users` held. What the contact was assigned to and stood behind goes with the inventory, and `hirdir admin load-examples` counts no such contact as inventory. A user given a contact, or one taken from it, is recorded on the contact as well, `user_hid` from one to the other, so that its history says so and a kept answer that shows an operator as internal or external is worked out again (P-157). The kind of an external system takes 40 characters, as a value of any list does. The upgrade gives a contact to the users that may sign in before those that are disabled, and of those alike to the first by username, so that of two users of one email the one still in use has it; it does not copy an email that a contact would refuse, not of the form name@host, and makes the contact without it; and the audit event of each user that had a display name or an email holds what was dropped, its display name and its email, with the contact it was given, so that the trail keeps what a service account, a second user of one email or a user whose name differed from its contact's lost. A user whose email is a retired contact's is given that contact, retired, as the contact is left as it is. A user with no email, or one a contact would refuse, is given the contact of its display name as a file finds a contact by its name: the one contact of that name, or of several the one with no email; where there is none, or it is another user's, a contact of its own is made, so that a user is not made a second contact of a name that one person already has. `hirdir admin load-examples` after a reset may change the contact of a user whose email is an example's, which the files of the examples match by its email; a reset after it keeps that contact as it then is. A problem with the new contact of `POST /users` names its field as `contact.email`, `contact.name`. The search of the users, `q`, goes through the dialect's comparison without regard to case. The throttle of signing in is kept by the name that was typed, so it does not follow a rename, which an Admin alone makes, and lasts an hour at most. A new person behind an operator is made by the page in two changes, the contact and then the operator, each audited. Why: The two reviews of the server of 0.9.3, on 2026-11-16: a reset refused by the contacts of users, kept answers that did not see a user given a contact, a column of 20 characters for a value of 40, a scope for an open label, what the upgrade did with two users of one email, an email that is no address and the values it dropped, and a second contact of one name made for a user with no email. These readings are mine; whether every role reads a username was the owner's. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.3. (Was P-174.) |
| D-242 | **A minor item's own groups come before the item it is in.** An item that has no value of its own for its location, its planned location, its operator or its owner walks up from itself one record at a time and takes the first value it finds: its own groups, ranked a stack before a cluster before a controller domain before a custom group and then by name and HID, each answering with its own value or that of the group it is nested in; then the item it is in, and that item's own groups; then the item that one is in and its groups, to the top; and, for the operator alone, then the place chain of the location in force. Until 0.9.4 only the groups of the item at the top of the line were looked at, and a minor item's own groups counted for nothing. The answer says where each value came from as before, `own`, `parent_item` or `device_group`, with the record that holds it. The lists of items filter, sort and count by the value in force worked out the same way, in the database. The rules of which group decides are written in one place, under "Groups, and which of them decides" on the data model page, with what may be in more than one group and what it has from them, for whoever runs hirdir or builds on it; and the row of the device groups that said a group's members each have their own location and owner says what D-129 made it. Nothing that is held changes: an item that is a minor item and a member of a group with a value may show another value in force after the upgrade, worked out when it is read, and a kept answer is worked out again when the service starts. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.3 was published, which records may be in more than one group, how inheritance is ranked and what breaks a tie, and asked for the rules to be written down for other teams; then asked that a minor item's own groups come first. Asked whether an optic in a switch with an operator of its own, and in a group with another, has its group's or its switch's, they chose its group's. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.4. (Was P-175.) |
| D-243 | **Why an item has each value: the walk, shown.** `GET /items/{hid}/resolution` answers, for each value that an item may have from elsewhere, the steps of its walk in their order, each with the record it looks at, `own`, `device_group`, `parent_item`, `catalog`, `setting`, `location`, `site` or `region`, with its HID and name, and a group's type and rank among the item's groups; the value that record holds, its own, or for a group from the group it is nested in, which is then named; and what the step is: `decides`, the first that holds a value, `passed_over`, one after it that holds another, `nothing`, one that holds none, or `retired`, an operator passed over as retired, or a retired group, which passes nothing down; and for a support weight, `raises`, the minimum that raises the value found, which keeps the source it was found at, as the record says it. A value of the item's own decides, and `overrides` names the first value beneath it, the value it would have without its own. The values are the four that come down the groups, `location_hid`, `planned_location_hid`, `operator_hid` and `owner_hid`, and the eight of the catalog, from its own to its catalog entry and, for `support_weight`, the setting. `GET /device-groups/{hid}/resolution` answers the same of a group: its own, then each group it is nested in. The page of an item and of a group show it under Values in force as a row of boxes, one for each step, joined by an arrow in the order of the walk: the box that decides drawn in the accent of the theme, one passed over muted with its value struck through, one with nothing dashed, each record a link; and the same as a table, for a screen reader and to copy. The work is done once, in the domain, by the same walk that the value in force is worked out by, so that the two cannot differ, and a test holds them together. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-17 whether a record opened for its detail lists all its relationships, which group decides and what is inherited and what overridden; the page listed the groups and the source of each value, but not why that group decided, what the others would have given, or what an override replaces. Boxes joined by arrows are what the owner asked for of a view across records. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.4. (Was P-176.) |
| D-244 | **What an operator is on, drawn.** `GET /operators/{hid}/assignments` answers what the label is on and how: the places given it as their own, each with its level and path, the items that have it from that place, the places beneath it that have it from that place, and the exceptions there, the items beneath it that have another operator; the device groups given it as their own, each with its type, the items that have it from that group, the groups nested in it that have it from that group, and the exceptions, the items under it at any depth of nesting that have another; the items given it as their own, and the minor items that have it from the item they are in; and the totals, which add up to the items it is in force for. Each count is of the items of the view as the scoreboard counts them, and comes with the filter of the item list that lists them: `effective.operator_hid.source` and `effective.operator_hid.source_hid` are fields of the item list, to filter and sort by, so that a box leads to exactly its items. The page of an operator draws it as a tree of boxes: the operator at the top, then a column each for its places, its groups and its own items, each box with its counts and joined to what it holds by a line, the exceptions marked, every count a link. A place or a group with more than twenty beneath it shows the first twenty and a link to the rest. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-17 for a view of the objects an operator is assigned; the page counted the items it is in force for and listed the places given it, but not the groups and places that have it from above, how each item has it, or what inside its groups and places is someone else's. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.4. (Was P-177.) |
| D-245 | **A leaderboard of operators and of groups.** The scoreboard's table of operators gains a rank, 1 for the most, by the column it is sorted by, two of one count sharing a rank; how its items have it, `own`, `from_item`, `from_group` and `from_place`, which add up to its items; and how many groups and places are given it as their own. Each count is drawn with a bar beside it, in the accent of the theme, as long as the count is to the largest of its column, the number always written beside it. The scoreboard gains a table of the device groups, ranked in the same way: each group with its type, its current members, the groups nested in it, and of the items under it, its members and the members of every group nested in it at any depth and the minor items of all of them, the items, the components, the managed nodes, the support weight, the components by health, and with `operators=true` the operators involved, each count a link to the item list. It is `by=group` on `GET /reports/summary`, a row for each device group that is not retired with its HID as the key and its name as the label, and the blank row the items in no group; an item in several groups is in the row of each, as one with several tags is, and the totals count it once, which the page says. The management report takes `by=group` too. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-17 for a leaderboard, a scoreboard and rankings of what is assigned to each operator and of what is within each group; the scoreboard ranked the operators by what they are in force for, and nothing counted a group but its own members. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.4. (Was P-178.) |
| D-246 | **Counts by model, and every count ranked.** `by=model` on `GET /reports/summary` and `GET /reports/management` gives a row for each catalog entry, keyed by its HID and labelled with its vendor and its PID, `Cisco C9300-48P`, beside its `vendor`, `pid`, `model_name`, `kind` and `type`: the models of every vendor in one table, so that they are ranked against each other, where the portfolio of a vendor lists the models of that vendor alone. A model is a vendor's product: `by=type`, as before, counts what is of one type whatever its vendor, all the switches together, and `by=model` with the filter `catalog.type:eq:switch` ranks the models of switch of every vendor. The blank row holds the items with no catalog entry. A catalog entry answers `items`, how many items of the view are of that model, counted as every count is (D-152): major and minor, drafts, retired records and items in Archived left out; `GET /catalog-entries` sorts by it, and the Catalog page shows it as a column, a link to those items. Every table of the summary, the management report and the scoreboard has a rank, from 1, by the column it is sorted by, two of one count sharing a rank and the blank row ranked with none, and a bar beside each count, as P-178 has it for the operators and the groups. Why: The owner asked on 2026-11-17 whether any view counts the items by manufacturer, by model and by site, and ranks the sites by their items: the summary and the management report did by vendor and by site, and the portfolio by the models of one vendor, but nothing ranked the models of every vendor or gave a rank. They chose the three, and asked whether by model meant by type; it does not, and both stand. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.4. (Was P-180.) |
| D-247 | **What the lanes of 0.9.4 chose where the pages were silent.** **A retired group passes nothing down.** A group nested in a retired group has nothing from it, and its members nothing through it, in the record, in the list and in the walk, where the walk shows the retired group as its last step, `retired`; a retired group still shows its own values on its own record. Until 0.9.4 a retired group passed its values down to the groups nested in it, while its own members, whose memberships its retiring ends, had none of them. **Under a group.** The item list has `group`, the groups an item is under, as a row of the summary by group holds them: `group:within:<HID or name>` lists the current members of that group and of every group nested in it at any depth, through no retired group, with the minor items of all of them, and `group:empty:true` the items under no group, so that each count of the table of groups leads to exactly its items. **The rows by group and by model.** A draft group has a row, as the item list's `group.hid` takes it; a group or a model with no item of the view has none, as with every `by`; `member_count` is of the current memberships whatever the view, and `nested_groups` of the groups that are not retired; the minor items under a group are at any depth; a model's `vendor` is its vendor's name, and its `type` and `kind` the values of the record. **The rows by operator** answer `own`, `from_item`, `from_group` and `from_place`, how its items have it, which add up to its items, and `groups_given` and `places_given`, on `GET /reports/summary` and `GET /reports/management`; they are null on the blank row and on a report by anything else. **The rank** is by the column a table is sorted by; where that is no count, or nothing, by its first count: the items of the summary and of the management report, the support weight of the scoreboard, which a note under the table says. Ties share a rank and the next skips, 1, 2, 2, 4; the blank row has none. A bar stands beside every count and every amount, the summary's totals by kind and by condition among them, and is not read aloud, as the number beside it is. The Summary and the Management report open sorted by their items, the most first, as the Scoreboard does; the order of the rows in the answer, which the command line prints and a file holds, is as it was. **What an operator is on** counts the minor items with the rest, and its links open the item list with minor items shown. **The Scoreboard, as the owner chose.** How each operator has its items, with the groups and places given it, is a table of its own beneath the leaderboard of operators, ranked, with bars and a file of its own; and the health of what an operator, a site or a group looks after is one column, a bar in four parts, good, degraded, failed and unknown, with the four numbers beside it, so that the leaderboard fits a window 1280 pixels wide; a file keeps the four columns. **The links of the leaderboard.** On the table of groups the items lead to `group:within` and the members to the group's members; the components, the managed nodes and the health lead nowhere, as no filter of the item list counts exactly what they count. On the table of operators the split leads to the item list by `effective.operator_hid.source`, `from_place` by its three levels, and the groups given to the list of groups by `operator_hid`. On the tree of an operator, the link to the rest of a place's or a group's twenty leads to that place or group. **The review of the server** found a file import that walked the old way, a report by group that failed when filtered by group, values in force that a walk at each level made several times slower to filter by, the management report by operator without its split, `members_disagree` that took a member's owner from its own line alone, the walk of a raised support weight, and two steps said to hold nothing that are retired; each is put right, the walk of each field ranking an item's groups once for every level. A second review of those corrections found a group of tens of thousands of members whose record the old `members_disagree` could not answer, the ranked table worked out again at each level by SQLite, a file that judged a row's operator before its new location, and the walk of a weight raised to the minimum that named the wrong value beneath its own; each is put right, `members_disagree` again one statement, by the owner in force of the item list, and the ranked table worked out once for each statement. **For PostgreSQL, when it comes** (after the MVP): a table that a statement reads more than once is kept whole by PostgreSQL and read again by each lookup of an item, and its order of names follows the collation of the database where the rest of hirdir sorts by code point; both are to be measured and settled when PostgreSQL is built. **Timing.** On 50,000 items the filters by a value in force, the summaries and the reports come back to about what 0.9.3 took; the page of what an operator is on takes from 2 to 6 seconds, counting each of its boxes, and goes to the timing of 1.0.0 with these numbers. Why: The lanes and the lead of 0.9.4, on 2026-11-17. The owner chose that the Summary and the Management report open most first, that how each operator has its items be a table of its own, and that health be one column so that the leaderboard fits. The rest are readings. Decided on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, as it stood and was built in 0.9.4. (Was P-181.) |

## Proposed

On 2026-11-17, after 0.9.4 was published, the owner confirmed every proposal that had been built, those that were the owner's answers and those that were readings of the lead's: P-05, P-06, P-07, P-08, P-09, P-10, P-11, P-12, P-13, P-14, P-15, P-16, P-17, P-18, P-19, P-21, P-48, P-58, P-139, P-140, P-141, P-142, P-145, P-146, P-147, P-148, P-149, P-150, P-151, P-152, P-153, P-154, P-155, P-156, P-157, P-158, P-159, P-160, P-161, P-162, P-163, P-164, P-165, P-166, P-167, P-168, P-169, P-170, P-171, P-172, P-173, P-174, P-175, P-176, P-177, P-178, P-180, P-181. They are D-190 to D-247, in that order, each as it stood and was built. P-143, P-144 and P-179, which are not built yet, stay proposed.

Each of these is the working answer. P-01 to P-04,
P-20, P-22, P-23 and P-24 were confirmed and are now D-33 to D-36 and D-40 to D-43. P-25 to P-27,
P-29 to P-31, P-33 to P-35 and P-37 to P-40 were answered on 2026-11-13 and are now
D-49 to D-61, three of them changed by the answer. P-28, P-32 and P-36 followed the same
day as D-62 to D-64. The proposals of release 0.3.0, P-41 to P-59, were answered on that day
too, but for two, and are D-65 to D-85: P-46 and P-59 were changed by the answer, and
D-70, D-74, D-75, D-77 and D-78 are decisions that the answers brought with them, the
reset, the setup tool that upgrades and the nightly timer among them. P-48 is left as
it is to be looked at again, and P-58 stands unanswered.

P-60 to P-77 are the choices of release 0.4.0, made where the other pages were silent
or disagreed with the build plan. Each is built. They are being answered one at a
time, from 2026-11-13: All of them were answered on 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14: P-60 to P-78 are D-86 to D-91,
D-93 to D-95, D-97 to D-101 and D-103 to D-107, six of them changed by their answers
(the sixth: a move to Released or Archived ends memberships): a stack may be its master alone, its master is an
observation, one switch alone earns no stack discount, a record has a primary
management address of each family, and the money has a report of its own. D-96, how a cluster is managed, came with P-68, and D-102, a shared address held by
the group and controllers found through nested clusters, with P-73. What the review of 0.4.1 then found unsaid was P-79 and is D-109, changed by its
answer: a moment that names no zone is in the application's time zone. The same for a
filter on a time was P-80 and is D-110. The answer brought D-92 with it, the condition
of an item and the health of a stack, and P-78 is the detail of that, for 0.4.1.

P-81 to P-88 are the choices of release 0.5.0, made where the other pages were silent
or did not agree with one another. P-89 is what the review of its server then found
unsaid. Each is built. They are being answered one at a time, from 2026-11-14: P-81 is
