D-114, P-82 is D-115, changed by its answer, which brought D-116 with it, P-83 is
D-117, P-84 is D-118, P-85 is D-119, changed by its answer, P-86 is
D-120, P-87 is D-121, P-88 is D-123 and P-89 is D-126. What P-89 said of a draft place
brought D-125 and D-127 with it, the planned location of an item and of a group, with
P-92 as their detail, and P-91 is a scoreboard that the owner asked for. P-91 is D-131, and its answer brought D-132, every table sorted by its headers. P-92 is D-133 and P-93 is D-134. P-90 is D-128,
and its answer brought D-129, inheritance down every hierarchy, with P-93 as its
detail. The answers to P-86 and P-88 brought D-122
and D-124 with them, the operator of an item, a group and a place, and P-90 is their
detail. P-94, now D-136, is what D-132 left to be said of the server: what a column of several
values, a count and a derived value are sorted by, and what each paged table takes.

P-95 to P-107 were the choices of release 0.6.0, made where the other pages named a
record and its fields and said no more. All of them were answered on 2026-11-14, while
the release was still being built, and are D-140 to D-151 and D-153, seven of them
changed by their answers: a contract past its period that still says active is
flagged; a coverage made by mistake is removed, and one is ended when its item leaves;
gear that is parked with the team is in service; a delivery may be received as a
count, and a request that has arrived in full says so by itself; a registration made
by mistake is removed, and a member shows what its stack is registered with; the items
under more than one contract are listed; and a renewal that is entered early leaves
the old term running, while one that is late has a status of its own. D-137 to D-139
were decided before them, in the same days: a contract kept in one place and renewed
there, what it says of how it was bought and whom to ask, and a support agreement that
groups contracts. D-152 is what the review of the server then showed of two counts. P-108 to P-110
are what the second review, of the answers as they were built, left for a person: they
were answered the same day and are D-154 to D-156, all three as they stood.

P-115 to P-126 were the proposals of 0.7.0, P-124 among them an idea for later, from
the timing of 0.6.1 and from the owner's wishes for renewals, the contacts of a
contract, purchase lines and settings; P-127 to P-138 were their particulars, written
when 0.7.0 was started, with six choices in them answered by the owner the same day and
what the two reviews of its server asked answered after. All of them were decided on
2026-11-15, after 0.7.0 was published, as they stood and were built: P-115 to P-138 are
D-166 to D-189, in the same order, P-124 an idea for later that is not built.

P-111 to P-114 were the choices of release 0.6.1, made where the corrections that the
build plan lists for it left the particulars to be said. They were answered on
2026-11-14, before they were built: P-111 is D-159, and P-112 is D-160, changed by
its answer, which gave an agreement a purchase history of its own. P-113 is D-161,
changed by its answer, which reports an item of another model than its line's, and
P-114 is D-162. What the review of the server then found brought D-163, which day of
the agreement an earlier term takes its dates from, and D-164, an agreement renewal
that renumbers its members, for 0.7.0. The timing of the release brought D-165, how the
response target is measured.

P-139 to P-144 were asked for by the owner on 2026-11-15, during 0.7.0 and after it was
published, for 0.8.0, 1.0.0 and after the MVP, P-141 to P-143 with their open points
answered the same day. P-145 to P-157 are the particulars of 0.8.0, written when it was
started on 2026-11-15, from six questions the owner answered that day: a part month of a
circuit is counted as the carrier bills it; the cost of a circuit counts at its A side
unless it is switched, with a setting for new circuits, and circuits are put into groups
whose cost is one sum; the account team and the support desk of a carrier are named once,
on the carrier; a far end outside the network has no location and a note; a circuit has a
type and a speed of named values; and Try changes the planning figures and the staff
lines as well as the defaults and minimums.

P-158 to P-163 are the particulars of 0.9.0, written when it was started on 2026-11-16,
from six questions and one follow-up that the owner answered that day: regions, sites,
locations, catalog entries, circuits and single devices take files; an administration
guide is uploaded once and attached to every model it covers; a place lists its own
files and then those of the places above it, each marked with where it is attached; a
file may be up to 100 MB; the kinds of file are a list that an Admin may change, with
pictures shown in the page and everything else downloaded, and web pages, SVG, scripts
and programs refused; and removing a file erases its content at once, the backups on
the host keeping their copy until they rotate out. P-164 is what the lanes and the
review of 0.9.0 chose where the pages were silent. P-165 is what the owner asked of
the restore when 0.9.0 was published, for 0.9.1, and P-166 the command that loads the
examples, for 0.9.1 too.

P-167 to P-169 are what the owner asked after 0.9.1 was published, for 0.9.2, written
from four answers given on 2026-11-16: a release of their own before 1.0.0; set by an
Admin in Admin › Settings and audited, not in the configuration file; the product name
in place of hirdir in all of the web page and in the names of downloaded files, the
commands and the API keeping theirs; and whom to ask shown on About, and on the sign-in
page only where an Admin turns that on.

P-170 is what the owner asked after 0.9.2 was published, for 0.9.3: the kinds of
external system made an editable list, as the kinds of location and of reference are,
and a system with no kind refused where `other` is not offered.

P-171 to P-173 are what the owner asked the same day, for 0.9.3 too, and P-174 is what the review of its server found: a setting changed
where it is listed, a username that is changed, and the people of the inventory. Two
answers settled the people: an outside person behind an operator is a contact, with a
second number, and every person is a contact, of which a user and an operator are each
a part that adds only what is its own, so that a part that comes later is added in
the same way. The owner added that a breaking change is acceptable before 1.0.0: an
upgrade may be disruptive and may lose data, and an installation under trial imports
its few records again.

P-175 to P-179 are what the owner asked on 2026-11-17, after 0.9.3 was published, for
0.9.4, and for after 1.0.0, P-180 what they asked after them, for 0.9.4, and P-181 what the lanes of 0.9.4 chose where the pages were silent: a minor item's own groups before the item it is in, the
rules of which group decides written in one place, the walk of each value shown, what an
operator is on drawn as boxes, a leaderboard of operators and of groups, counts by model
and a rank on every report, and the views of the data to come after the MVP. Asked which of an optic's operators decides, its
group's or its switch's, the owner chose its group's; asked which of four went into
0.9.4, all four.

| ID | Proposal | Why | Built in |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-143 | **Imports at speed, within their targets.** An import of 10,000 rows previews in under 30 s and applies in under 60 s, as [requirements.md](requirements.md#qualities) says; the targets are kept. At 0.7.0 it took 122 s and 168 s: some 13.6 ms a row, most of it in finding the row's places, operators and model again for every row, and 199 pauses of 0.12 s between the batches of an apply, some 24 s. Each row is made cheaper without changing what an import does: the places, the operators and the models a file names are looked up once for the file and held while it is read, and a batch of an apply pauses only while another request waits for the database's one writer, as a sign-in does, and not after every batch. What a preview and an apply decide of each row, the role checked in each batch, a stop and a resume (D-185) stay as they are. `make time-import` measures it again in the timing of 1.0.0. | The owner answered on 2026-11-15, asked how 1.0.0 should tackle the speed of imports: make each row cheaper and keep the targets. | 1.0.0 |
| P-144 | **A colour theme for the web page, chosen by each person, after the MVP.** A person who opens hirdir sees the default theme: what the page shows now, light or dark as the device is set. A menu at the top of every page offers the others, and the choice is kept in the browser's local storage under `hirdir.theme`, never in a cookie, as no response sets one. The choice belongs to the browser, not to the account: a person who signs in on another browser sees the default there until they choose again. Where local storage is blocked the choice lasts for the page only. Choosing a theme writes nothing to the server and is not audited, as it changes no record. The themes are those of the owner's Texas2Step project, taken from it rather than drawn again; at the time of writing they are Match system, Light, Dark, America, Energy, Federal, Military and Texas. The saved theme is applied before the page is first painted, by a small script file in the head of the page, as the content security policy of hirdir allows no inline script. Every theme passes the contrast checks of the accessibility pass, the grid's colours among them. **The themes are taken as they stand only if that is straightforward when the release is started.** At the time of writing it is not, for three reasons. Texas2Step's theme blocks sit in its one stylesheet beside rules for its own page, its top bar and its backdrop. Its colours are named for its own page (`--page`, `--ink`, the colours of its charts and the metals of its gauges), while hirdir's are named `--bg`, `--text`, `--accent`, `--danger`, `--ok` and the like. And its menu is built with Texas2Step's own page helpers. If that is still so, the agent reminds the owner to make the themes of Texas2Step easy to reuse first, as a file of themes alone whose colours are named for what each is for, before hirdir takes them. Whether an Admin may set another default theme for the site, as Texas2Step can, is settled when the release is started. | The owner asked for it on 2026-11-15, for after the MVP: a theme of the person's choosing, kept in the browser and not in a cookie, from the themes of Texas2Step, with a reminder to make them reusable there if they cannot be taken as they stand. | after 1.0.0 |
| P-179 | **Views of the data, after the MVP.** Ways of looking at what hirdir holds besides its lists, each another view of a list and never a page apart, built after 1.0.0 in about this order: a pivot of the summary by any two of its dimensions, as site by lifecycle state or vendor by coverage, with any of its measures, exported as a file; a treemap of the geography, regions, sites and locations each as large as its components or its support weight and coloured by what no contract covers or by health, and one of vendors and models coloured by how near their end of life is; heatmaps of operators by sites, the scoreboard as a grid, of sites by the month their contracts end, and of vendors by the year of end of life; bar charts of the end of life by year and vendor, of capacity, demand beside supply, by category and by region, of the cost of circuits by carrier, and of the sections of the coverage report; a bubble chart of the sites, their components against their support weight per engineer, each as large as its support or circuit cost; flows of the items through their lifecycle states, with the moves of the last 30 or 90 days read from the audit trail, of purchases from requested to in service, and of renewals from ending to renewed or lapsed; and drawings of a controller domain with its clusters, controllers and endpoints, of a stack with its members, and of a circuit between its two sides with its carriers. A pie is kept for a whole of four parts or fewer, and a stacked bar is preferred to it. **So that the project stays one thing:** every view is drawn by hirdir itself, in SVG, by one small set of components and in the colours of the theme alone, as the capacity balance is drawn, so that it follows a theme of P-144 and needs no library; every view has its numbers as a table beside it, labels a screen reader reads, and passes the checks of the accessibility pass; every mark, bar or cell is a link to the item list or report filtered to what it counts; and every view takes the filter, the drafts and the retired of the list it is of, and is kept as the report it is drawn from is. | The owner asked on 2026-11-17 which of what hirdir holds lends itself to pivot tables, treemaps, pie charts, bar charts, bubble charts and heatmaps, and to boxes and lines across workflows and groups, keeping the layout, the page and the theme consistent, and asked for those to be on the build plan for after 1.0.0. | after 1.0.0 |
| P-182 | **What a page says after a change stays in view.** What a page answers to a change, that it was done or why it was refused, is shown where it always was, and while the window is scrolled past that place it stays at the top of the window, over the page, until the page is scrolled back to it. It is still part of the page and is read out as before; it is not a message that goes away by itself. A message that something was done is taken away when the next change on that page is sent, so that a page never says done and refused at once. | The owner asked on 2026-11-17 for a review of the web UI for what costs its users clicks, scrolling or doubt; this was measured on a scratch server with the examples at 1280 by 800. After Save at the foot of an item's page the window stood 7,700 px down and "The item was saved." was 356 px from the top, out of sight; the same on the pages of a group, a circuit and a site, on Settings and on Choices: every page that answers through the shared notice. An earlier "saved" stayed beside a later refusal. | 0.9.5 |

| P-187 | **Plain words find a record.** In the filter box above a list, text that holds no condition of the short form is taken as words: a record matches when each word is held by one of the fields that list is known by (an item: its name, host name, serial number, asset tag and address; a contact: its name and email; a catalog entry: its PID and model; and so for each list). It is sent as the tree of the filter grammar, which the server already takes; nothing changes in the API. Text that is neither says how that list's own short form reads, with that list's own fields. Owners has the box as the other lists do. The menu bar has one box, Find an item, on every page: what is typed there opens the list of items with those words, minor items among them. Amended after the review of 0.9.5: text the list cannot read is never sent to it, where it showed every item, but said under the box; a list opened with such text says why at once and asks for no row while it is in force, so that nothing acts on the whole inventory; the same words asked again make the list anew; a word of hex digits parted by colons, dots or hyphens, as a MAC or an IPv6 address, is a word whatever its first group is; an item is found by its MAC address for a word that can be one, and only then, since the server refuses other text there; and double quotes round a word are dropped. | The owner asked on 2026-11-17 for a second review of the web UI by what the network operations team does each day: finding an item and what is near it, adding an item, changing an item, finding a site or a location, the items and the contacts of a location, and counts. Measured on scratch servers at 1280 by 800, one of them with some 47,000 fictional items. There was no box to search in: "sw-01274" typed above the items was refused with the grammar's help, and finding a switch by its host name took `hostname:contains:sw-01274`, 26 keys and the name of the field. | P-199 | **The browser scenarios on the host: one run that reports every failure.** One command makes a fresh scratch inventory, serves the tree it is run in, runs the ordinary scenarios and then each project that runs alone, whatever failed before it, and ends with one list of every scenario that failed. The scenarios of the geography are in files that need nothing of each other. | P-217 | **A switch that joins a stack can take the stack's name and place in the same step.** A member added to a stack may be added with `drop_identity`: in the one change the item's own host name and FQDN are cleared, its addresses are removed as Remove removes them, and its own location is cleared, so that the stack's is in force; and with a `disposition` and `expected_online` for it, which the lifecycle rule of its state allows or refuses as for any change. Each is audited as a change of the item, made by joining that stack. It is for a stack alone: the members of a cluster and of a controller domain keep their names. The web's form to add a member to a stack has a box for it, ticked from the start, which a person unticks for a member that keeps a name of its own, and it then asks for Production where the item is a spare; the same is a column of the file of members. Nothing of the stack is copied to the member: its page shows the stack's host name, FQDN and addresses as the stack's, read from the stack (P-222). As built: what an audit event came from is an entry of its changes, `joining_group` with the group's HID as its new value, since an event has no column for a cause. The member's own location is cleared whether or not the stack has one in force. The first members of a stack made by New group are not offered the box. The owner confirmed on 2026-11-18 that the member's own location is cleared when it joins even where the stack has none: by default the stack has the location and the switch inherits it, not the other way round, and an operator may still give a member a location of its own over the stack's. | The owner asked on 2026-11-17 to make it easier to add and remove switches in a stack, for an organization with hundreds of stacks and a couple of thousand switches, most of them in stacks, with "a fair bit of churn each week adding a switch to a stack and occasional removals or replacements"; a stack is known by the stack's name and its members are "generally not individually addressed", and the team reaches "for the stack as a group of switches" first. Measured on a scratch inventory of that shape, 386 stacks and 1,843 fictional switches, at 1280 by 800. The owner: "When a spare goes into a stack it drops its name and IP address and takes on the name and address of the stack, consider blank and inherit?". After Add, four clicks, a spare kept its host name, its depot as its location, which passed over the stack's room, and Spare; putting that right took another page, 8 clicks and 3 saves on a page 8.8 windows tall, with nothing to say it was wanted. Asked how the names should be blanked, the owner added: "Recycle bin, context-aware log or queue of things removed, maybe add now or wait until after 1.0.0, as names and addresses removed benefit from a hook to update IPAM and DNS systems, remove the standalone switch from monitoring tools" (answered on 2026-11-18: P-223). | 0.9.6 |
| P-218 | **A member that leaves a stack is given what becomes of it in the same step.** Ending a membership may carry what the item is to have from that day: a host name and an FQDN, a location, a lifecycle state with its reason, a disposition and whether it is expected online, each checked by the item's own rules and audited as its change, in the one change with the end. The web's form to end a membership of a stack asks What becomes of it, a person's choice among: to the spare pool, with a new name and a place; to be returned to the manufacturer; excess; or nothing now. Each fills the fields the server is sent, which the person may change; no state follows from the end by a rule. The answer names the item as a link, and the row that ended stays in view until the page is left. As built: it is taken for a group of any type; a state the item already has is no move; and the condition of the item is not among what the end carries, so that a failed member is marked failed on its own page. The event's entry is `leaving_group`. After its review: what becomes of the item is given with an end on a day that has come, since it is done with the request, and a day to come is refused with it; and `leaving_group` is an entry of the item's own events alone, not of what follows from a move of the item, as the end of its other memberships. The owner confirmed on 2026-11-18 that it stays for groups of every type. | The owner asked on 2026-11-17 to make it easier to add and remove switches in a stack, for an organization with hundreds of stacks and a couple of thousand switches, most of them in stacks, with "a fair bit of churn each week adding a switch to a stack and occasional removals or replacements"; a stack is known by the stack's name and its members are "generally not individually addressed", and the team reaches "for the stack as a group of switches" first. Measured on a scratch inventory of that shape, 386 stacks and 1,843 fictional switches, at 1280 by 800. The owner: a member pulled that did not fail "'ll go to the spare pool with a new name and address", and a failed one "is either processed via RMA for replacement by the manufacturer or processed for disposal/excess as bad hardware". Sending a member back to the pool took 14 clicks and 48 keys: the ended row vanished, to be brought back by Show past memberships, and until it was given a place the item had none, having had the stack's. | 0.9.6 |
| P-219 | **A position in a stack is held by one member.** Adding a member to a stack at a position that another current member holds, and moving one to such a position, is refused, naming the member that holds it; the stacks that hold two at one position already stay as they are until one is changed. The position offered for a new member is the lowest that is free, so that a replacement takes the place of the one that left; a row of a file of members that gives a stack no position takes the lowest free one. As built: positions are numbered from 1, in a stack alone; `next_position` of the group is the lowest free one; a replacement hands its position over as it stands. After its review: a file of members is judged when all its rows are in, so that it swaps two members or renumbers a stack, and a row that leaves its member where another still is is rejected then; by the API two members change places by way of a free position or of none. | The owner asked on 2026-11-17 to make it easier to add and remove switches in a stack, for an organization with hundreds of stacks and a couple of thousand switches, most of them in stacks, with "a fair bit of churn each week adding a switch to a stack and occasional removals or replacements"; a stack is known by the stack's name and its members are "generally not individually addressed", and the team reaches "for the stack as a group of switches" first. | P-223 | **What a change removes is worked from the audit trail, after 1.0.0.** A name or an address that a change removes, as when a spare joins a stack and drops its own, is not put in a recycle bin or a queue by the change: the removal, like every addition and change, is an audit event that says what was removed, from which record, and what it came from (P-217, P-218). A feature after 1.0.0 reads the audit trail, by a trigger, on demand or both, and decides from each event whether a name or an address needs onboarding, offboarding or a change in an external system, the IPAM, the DNS, the monitoring tools; where it is turned on, the events may feed a queue of those actions. It is the audit event that is evaluated: no hook is added at each place in the application where a person may make such a change. The updates go out in batches after the changes, not as they happen; whether nearer to real time is wanted is to be weighed then, and it may come with a wizard or a workflow that walks a person through the process. Until then 0.9.6 does no more than keep the events. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, asked when the recycle bin, log or queue of removed names and addresses of 2026-11-17 should be built: "After 1.0.0 … removes, adds, and changes are audit events, and a feature to be built after 1.0.0 will read from the audit trail that a hook/trigger and/or on-demand feature parses to determine what addresses and/or names may need processing through onboarding, offboarding, or update/change with external systems; when enabled the audit events may feed a queue for these actions, where it's the audit event that is evaluated to detrmine if an entry is added to the queue, not adding a hook everywhere in the application a user may make a change that involves this type of follow-on integration with the external systems; batch updates after changes are performed not in real-time, or near real-time to be evaluated, may combine with a wizard or workflow feature that walks through the process". The box of P-217, ticked from the start, was put to the owner with the question and stands. | after 1.0.0 |

The mark is the item's, so it holds for every contract that covers the item, and the page says so before it is set. | P-226 | **A thing that runs on another is an item with a host.** A virtual device context in a switch, a virtual machine and a long-lived container are items of their own, each seen on the network by a name and an address, linked to what they run on: an item, or a group where the hosts are a cluster, since a virtual machine or a container may run on a cluster. The link is the reverse of a membership of a stack: the item keeps its own name and address, and by default has from its host its location, its owner and its operator, each resolved when read and never copied down, which an operator may override. It has an intended host, which a person names, and an observed host, which a tool reports, as a stack has an intended and an observed master. Such items count as components, as managed nodes and in the support weight. An item of a kind that runs on a host may be entered with none, and is then marked for review. When a host is retired, what runs on it is retired with it, the web saying first what that takes with it. Short-lived application containers are not `hirdir`'s to sort out: the system that pushes what it discovers decides which containers it sends, and what it sends is tracked. Settled by the owner the same day: this is unlike a stack on purpose, whose retirement ends its memberships and leaves its switches as they are, since a virtual device context cannot be without its switch; an item that runs on a cluster stays with the cluster when one host of the cluster is retired; a virtual device context has no serial number of its own and shows its host's, as from its host; and what a tool pushes is matched by its identifier in that tool, a registration with an external system, so that a rename or a move makes no second record, and an item the tool reports no more is marked for review and never retired by `hirdir` on its own. Its particulars are P-246 to P-250, with what the owner answered when 0.9.8 was begun: retiring the record of a cluster that something runs on is refused; matching by a tool's identifier and the mark of what it reports no more are both built in 0.9.8; and a replacement of a host asks in its form whether what runs on it goes to the new one. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, after 0.9.6: "Switches going into a stack is a good model, now how to do the reverse, where virtual machines go into a hypervisor, or switch Virtual Device Context (VDC) go into Cisco Nexus switch?"; "Physical objects, tangible objects (things that cause humans to do some level of work when managing the network, such as anything with an IP address and/or DNS name, which includes containers and virtual machines which aren't physical), and things that cost support effort and/or cost dollars (contracts, circuits, etc.) all need handling inside this tool"; "List as an item is maybe the most straightforward as it is seen on the network with a management address, but link it with a switch, so they move together"; and, to six questions: "virtual machines and containers can run on a clusters"; "Values from the host by default to inherit for running-on members include: location, owner, operator"; "intended host and observed host are good for running-on members, as a stack has intended and observed master"; "virtual machines and containers count as components, managed nodes and support weight"; "When a host is retired, its running-on members should be retired, as a stack is retired with its member switches, yes, possibly with a warning if the action is performed via web interface?"; "System containers, long-lived, should be tracked much like virtual machines, while short-lived application containers will come and go from the environment ... Consider leaving this for the external system that pushes data into hirdir to be responsible for filtering out short-lived containers". And to what the lead then put: "Yes to recommendation for answer 5, VDC cannot exist without its Nexus, stacks are handled different on purpose versus hosted items. Agreed, when hosted on a cluster the virtual machine or container stay with the cluster when a cluster host is retired. Yes, VDC should show serial number from its host. Agreed on recommendations for answer 6." | 0.9.8 |
| P-227 | **An Admin deletes a record that was a mistake.** Retiring stays the way a record ends; deleting removes one that should never have been, as the items a wrong import made. Only an Admin deletes, and the server checks it. In 0.9.7 an item and a device group are deleted; every other kind is retired as before, and deleting those is on the list of what follows 1.0.0, at the owner's word. Only a record that nothing else depends on is deleted: what belongs to it alone goes with it, an item's addresses, tags, memberships and registrations, a group's memberships and addresses; where another record depends on it, a contract that covers the item, a purchase line it arrived on, an item installed in it, a replacement it was part of, a circuit connected to it, the deletion is refused and says what refers to it, to be taken away first. Every deletion is an audit event of its own, with who, when, the reason the Admin gave and the whole record as it was; the events the record had before stay, and nothing is ever removed from the audit trail. It is done from the list of the records an import made, which a past import now has, where Inventory retires and an Admin deletes; from the rows selected in a list; and from the record's page; each asks first and says how many. A HID that was deleted is not given again. The standing rule of `CLAUDE.md` reads: Items are retired or archived; only an Admin deletes one, and every deletion is audited. | The owner, on 2026-11-18: "add a feature for an admin to delete an object, such as the use case where an operator with inventory access imports a bunch of information that creates objects that turns out to be incorrect, and then imports a second time with corrected information that creates more objects, where the first set of items now needs to be deleted. Admin access to delete include audit trail." Asked one question at a time, the owner chose: the deletion and the history both kept in the audit trail; only what nothing depends on; items and device groups first; and the shorter wording of the rule. | 0.9.7 |
| P-228 | **The rows ticked in a list are retired at once.** The lists of items and of device groups, and the list of the records an import made, have a button, Retire, for the rows ticked, which asks first and says how many. It is for particular rows and is not offered for all that match a filter, so that a wide filter retires nothing by one press. It is for the Inventory role, as retiring a record is; what is retired is hidden as before, shown again with archived records on, and brought back as before. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, asked whether a person with the Inventory role should retire many records at once from a list, for a wrong import of forty switches with no Admin at hand to delete them: "Yes, for selected rows". A record was retired one at a time on its page, or by a file with its status; a bulk edit does not retire. | 0.9.7 |
| P-229 | **The coordinates of a place open a map.** The latitude and longitude that a site and a location hold, shown as numbers on the place's page, are a link that opens a map in a new tab. A setting holds the address of the map with the place of the latitude and the longitude in it, Google Maps from the start; an installation sets another map, or blanks the setting, and the coordinates are then plain numbers, as where there is no access to the internet. Following the link sends the coordinates of that place to the map's site, and nothing else. Choosing a place from a map is on the list of what follows 1.0.0. | The owner, on 2026-11-18: "Recommend adding map coordinates as data fields for a location GPS Latitude Longitude, and a future post-1.0.0 feature to select location from Google Maps." The fields were there already, on sites and locations, shown as numbers; asked which map a link should open, the owner chose a setting, Google Maps to start. | 0.9.7 |
| P-230 | **A page that cannot be drawn says so, and offers to load again.** Where drawing a page fails, the page says in words that it could not be drawn, shows the text of the fault for whoever is asked about it, with a button that copies it, and offers two ways on: Load again, and the first page. The menu bar stays where the fault is in the page beneath it, and going to another page clears it. A part of the web page that cannot be fetched says the same, as where a release installed meanwhile has taken the old part away. Before its script runs the page holds words too: the product's name and that it is loading, and, where that has stood for a few seconds, that the page should be loaded again, so that a script which did not arrive leaves words and not an empty window. The fault is written to the browser's console and is sent nowhere. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, of the first days of use: "the browser screen sometimes goes blank when an operator has the page open to hirdir, opens different tabs for other web sites, clicks to the hirdir tab, then clicks away, clicks back later and the hirdir display is blank, black screen on dark theme, no text", in Microsoft Edge on Windows 11; and, to what the lead recommended: "Agreed on the recommended workarounds to suggest a reload on error and reporting new server version." Measured before anything was built, in a headless Chromium on the build host: with a tab resting on each of 43 pages of a published release and the server moved to a later one underneath, for four pairs of releases, no tab was empty on coming back and none after a press, since the page fetches its other pages ahead; a session that had ended brought the sign-in page; the error page of a proxy and no network at all left every page standing. Two ways to the empty dark window were found: an answer of another shape than the page expects emptied 7 to 21 of 38 pages on coming back, nothing catching the fault; and the page's own script not arriving left a window with no words. What happened in the owner's browser is therefore not known, and may be the browser's own; both ways that were found are closed. As built: the fault page stands beneath the menu bar, and around the whole page for a fault in the frame itself; any move clears it, to the same page too; a part that could not be fetched is fetched again when its page is next opened; and the words to load again come after six seconds. The reviewer who had not built it ran the measurements again on the release: no page of 37 was empty for any of the answers, and the script not arriving left the name and Loading. | 0.9.7 |
| P-231 | **A page older than the server says that a new version is installed.** The web page knows the version it was built as. On coming back to its tab, at a change of page when it last asked more than five minutes before, and when a page could not be drawn, it asks the server what is running, `GET /about`, which every signed-in person may read. Where the server's version is another than the page's, a notice stands on every page until the page is loaded again: that a new version has been installed, and a button, Load again. The page never loads again by itself, so that what is typed into a form is not lost. No header says the version, and nobody who is not signed in is told it. | The owner's agreement of P-230. A server upgraded each night under tabs that are left open is the daily case of this installation; the measurements of P-230 found no fault from it in four pairs of releases, and an old page is still not to go on unseen: what it sends and reads is of the release before. The owner confirmed on 2026-11-18 that the page asks, and that no header says the version. As built: the notice stands under the menu bar and on the fault page; the page's loading counts as having asked, and over ten changes of page nothing was asked. | 0.9.7 |
| P-232 | **Every value of a list that names a record is a link.** In the lists and the tables of the web page, a value that names another record opens that record's page: the stack or the group of an item first, then its location, its site, its region, its owner, its operator, its vendor and its model, the item a minor item is in, and likewise the contract, the agreement, the carrier, the place, the contact and the group that a row of any other list names. Such a link looks like the text around it, in the colour of the text and with no underline, and is underlined while the pointer is on it and while it has the keyboard's focus; the name that opens a row's own record stays the link it is. A press on a link does not select the row. Where a list's answer does not hold the HID of what a value names, the server adds it. What is copied and exported from a list is unchanged. | The owner, on 2026-11-18: "Keep the stack column as built, on the item list by default and can be turned off with the column selector. Make the stack name listed a link that can clicked taking the user to the stack page for that stack. Prefer doing that will all of the listed values, such as location, site, etc. Consider not highlighting with an underline as normal link but still allow clicking on the value as a hyperlink, just a CSS change maybe?" As built: in a list Ctrl or the middle button on such a link opens it in a new tab, as a browser does, and Enter on a cell opens the record it names, the first where it names several; a purchase line opens its request, having no page of its own; a network and a tag have no page and stay text; the server added the HID of the model to an item named by another record and of the vendor to a contract named so. The User column of the list of contacts is not a link, since a user's page is an Admin's alone: for the owner to say. | 0.9.7 |
| P-233 | **Rows are ticked.** A list whose rows are selected has a column of boxes at its left, one to a row, and one in the heading that ticks every row on show and clears them. What acts on the rows selected, Copy, Export, Bulk edit, and Retire and Delete of P-228 and P-227, says how many are ticked. Selecting by the row itself stays as it was. Typing into the cells of a list, the live edit the owner described, is with the cell ranges of the grid on the list of what follows 1.0.0, since each cell would need the rules, the inherited values and the refusal of a stale edit that a record's page has. | The owner, on 2026-11-18: "Checkboxes to the left when selecting rows are useful ... and a live edit mode where fields that are easy to change can be typed in the live grid view ... if the edit mode is a heavy effort, put on the list for post-1.0.0." The build plan already lists cell-range selection and editing in the grid after the MVP (R10.4), where the owner confirmed on 2026-11-18 that the live edit stays. As built, with what its review changed: the box in the heading ticks the rows read so far, a hundred at a time, says so with their number, and shows the half state unless every row of the list is read and ticked; all that match stays its own button. Pressing a row ticks it or unticks it and unticks nothing else, where a press had selected that row alone: with boxes on show one stray press cleared every tick, and since most cells of a row are links, Ctrl with a press opens a link in a new tab where it had added the row. It was the lead's decision of 2026-11-18, which the owner agreed to the same day, after 0.9.7 was published: "Agreed on your one decision to confirm - pressing a row ticks or unticks that row alone, and shift takes the rows between without unticking anything." The owner added, to consider in the future: "ticking in a row selects the row rather than the checkbox, but requires colors or other visual indicator if the checkbox isn't displayed", which is on the list of what follows 1.0.0. Shift with a press, or with Space, ticks the rows from the one last pressed to this one and unticks nothing: the grid's own Shift, which a second review found to untick every row outside the range after an untick, is kept out. Rows between that were not read yet cannot be ticked, and the list says so. The column of boxes is 24 pixels wide, so that the columns the owner asked to see at 1280 pixels are still in view. | 0.9.7 |
| P-234 | **The records an import made are listed with the import.** A past import opens the list of the records it made: for each the row of the file, what kind of record it is, its name as a link, and what it is now, active, a draft, retired or deleted. The vendors and the catalog entries that the import made only because a row named them are listed after them, each a link. For an import of items or of device groups the rows are ticked, and the rows ticked are retired by the Inventory role (P-228) or deleted by an Admin (P-227). It is `GET /imports/{hid}/created`, and `hirdir imports created`. Nothing new is stored: a row of an applied import keeps the HID of the record it made, and the audit trail the request that made the others. A page of the documentation, Fixing a wrong import, says what to do in each case: the preview's counts before applying; wrong values on the right records, corrected by exporting the rows with their HIDs and importing them back, or by a bulk edit, which also gives many items another model at once; the kind of a model, a switch or a router, changed once on its catalog entry for every item of it; and records that should never have been, retired or deleted from this list. | The owner, on 2026-11-18: "add a feature for an admin to delete an object, such as the use case where an operator with inventory access imports a bunch of information that creates objects that turns out to be incorrect, and then imports a second time with corrected information that creates more objects, where the first set of items now needs to be deleted"; and "what is the best way for an operator with inventory permissions to fix data errors like this example, and can an object PID or Kind (switch, router, etc.) be changed on an item in bulk?" There was no list of what an import made, and no undo of one. The owner confirmed on 2026-11-18 that the vendors and catalog entries made in passing are listed and are not deleted in 0.9.7. As built: What it made stands on an import that was applied or stopped, a hundred rows to a page, ticks kept across its pages; a bulk edit that made a model lists it there too. The review found that an import listed what another had made where two applies shared a request, as all those of the loaded examples did; an import now lists what its own apply made. | 0.9.7 |
| P-235 | **A place is found by its code, wherever its path is taken.** A step of the path of a place is the name of the region, the site or the location, as before, or its code where no name of that step matches. A path need not begin at the top: its first step may be the code of a region or of a site, which no two have, so `S2 > Building 1 > Room 102` finds what `Americas > Central > Site-2 > Building 1 > Room 102` finds; and after a site, a step that is the code of a location of that site finds it at whatever depth, since no two locations of one site have one code: `S2 > R102`. That holds in a file, in a filter and in a command alike, which take a place by one rule. An export writes the paths of names as before, and with `place_codes` writes each place the short way, by the code of its site and its own where it has them, which loads back to the same place. | The owner, on 2026-11-18: "When importing and exporting items, can location field match on the short Code as an option versus the full location Name?" A site has a code, and a location may have one, unique in its site; a file found a place by its HID or by its path of names alone. The option of the export was the lead's reading of "and exporting", which the owner confirmed on 2026-11-18. As built: after a location, too, the code of a location beneath it finds it; the name a row gives its own place is a name and never a code; an export by codes writes the names where the short way would not find the same place again; and Places by code is a box beside Copy and Export. A code that a region and a site both have finds neither as the first step of a path: whether such a code is refused when it is set is for the owner to say. | 0.9.7 |
| P-236 | **How several records are retired and deleted, and what a deletion takes with it.** `POST /items/retire` and `POST /device-groups/retire` take up to 1,000 HIDs, for the Inventory role, and retire each as its page does, with an audit event each; the answer says how many were retired, how many were retired already, and which were refused, each with the reason. `POST /items/delete` and `POST /device-groups/delete` take up to 1,000 HIDs and a reason, which is required, for an Admin; each record is judged by itself, and the answer says which were deleted and which were refused, each with what depends on it, by kind, HID and name. A record that only records of the same request depend on is deleted with them, as a switch with the power supplies in it. With `dry_run` the answer is the same and nothing is changed: it is what the web shows before it asks. With an item go its addresses, its tags, its memberships of groups, with the mark of master it had there, and its registrations; it is refused while a contract covers or has covered it, it arrived on a purchase line, an item is installed in it, it replaced or was replaced by another, a circuit is connected to it, or a reference file is attached to it, which is the safest reading of what belongs to it alone. With a device group go its memberships, its addresses, its tags and its registrations; it is refused while a group is nested in it or a circuit is connected to it. The audit event, `delete`, holds every field of the record as it was, what went with it, and the reason. A HID that was deleted answers `404`, and the answer says that the record was deleted, when and by whom. A place's answer has `map_url`, which the server makes of the setting `map_link` and the place's latitude and longitude, and which is null where either is missing: the setting is an address with `{latitude}` and `{longitude}` in it, `https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query={latitude},{longitude}` from the start, or blank (P-229). | What P-227, P-228 and P-229 leave to be chosen when they are built. The owner confirmed on 2026-11-18 that an item with a reference file attached is refused until the file is taken off. As built: the reason is not asked of a dry run; each address, membership and registration that goes with a record has an event of its own that names the record, and a stack whose master the item was seen to be is cleared of it by an event that says so; a coverage that has ended refuses as one in force does; the `404` of a deleted record has `deleted`, with when, by whom and why; at most 1,000 are ticked or named in a command at a time, and more are refused in words before anything is asked of the server; and the setting takes a web address with no user or password in it, both places after its host, and no quote or angle bracket. A host that is no host, a bracket with no address in it or a port that is no number, is refused in the same words, where a second review found it to answer 500. An item that is part of a replacement can never be deleted, since nothing removes a replacement: whether an Admin removes one that a wrong file made is for the owner to say. The reviewer of the server found no fault in deleting: after it no row of any table named the record but the audit trail and the import that made it. | 0.9.7 |
| P-237 | **A code that a region has is refused for a site, and a site's for a region.** Setting a code on a region that a site already has, or on a site that a region has, is refused, naming the record that has it, by the page, the API and a file alike; records that hold one code already stay as they are until one of them is changed. A path that begins with a code then leads to one place. After 1.0.0 the codes of places are looked at again: whether a code may be shared, a location found before a site and a site before a region, so that only two of one kind with one code collide. The lead's note to that: no two sites and no two regions have one code today, and two locations may only in different sites, so under that order the one tie left is between locations of two sites, which is why a location's code is taken after its site. As built: a region and a site that hold one code from before stay so, and saving either with the code it holds goes through. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, asked what should happen where a region and a site have one code, which a path beginning with it then finds neither of (P-235): "Refuse it for now. Roadmap, post-1.0.0 revisit the topic. If locations are searched by code, location wins over site, site wins over region, and only in a tie such as two locations with the same code, two sites with the same code, two regions with the same code, is there a conflict, yes or no? Haven't wargamed this enough to decide. Open to recommendations. Refuse for now if no further effort is warranted on this feature before the 1.0.0 release. Preferably all codes are unique, but that's a rigid requirement to place on all enterprises." | 0.9.8 |
| P-238 | **An Admin removes a replacement that was a mistake.** A replacement that a wrong file or a wrong press recorded is removed by an Admin, with a reason, and the audit event holds the replacement as it was; the two items are then as any other, and are deleted or kept by the rule of P-227. Nothing else removes a replacement. It is `POST /item-replacements/{hid}/remove`, with `reason`, which is required: the memberships and the connections of circuits that the replacement handed over stay as they are, each a fact of its own that is put right on the page of its group or its circuit; both items have an event of it in their history; and the page of an item offers it to an Admin on each line of its replacements, asking first. As built: the answer is `204`; the Admin's reason is `removal_reason` in the events, a replacement having a reason of its own; each of the two items has an event `replacement_removed`. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, asked whether an Admin should remove a replacement that never happened, since an item that is part of one can never be deleted (P-236): "Yes, an Admin removes it". A coverage and a registration that were a mistake are removed so already. | 0.9.8 |
| P-239 | **The username in the list of contacts is a link for an Admin.** An Admin sees the User column of the list of contacts as a link to the user's page; everyone else sees the username as text, as before, since the page is an Admin's alone. As built: who made a change, in the audit trail and in the history of an item, is such a link too. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, asked what the one column should do that 0.9.7 left unlinked (P-232): "A link for an Admin only". | 0.9.8 |
| P-240 | **The tables that were not looked at one by one are.** The tables of the scoreboard, of the totals of the reports, of the settings and of the audit trail are gone through for values that name a record and are not links, the record of an audit event first, where a record that was deleted stays text. As built: a script listed, on 33 pages, each cell of a table that holds the name of a record its page's answers have the HID of and is no link: the rows of the summary and of the management report by vendor, by owner, by site and by region, 26 cells, and who made a change in the audit trail, which are links now; a row by tag stays text, a tag having no page. The record of an audit event that an event on show deleted is text, said to be deleted; where the deletion is not on show the link leads to the page that says it was deleted. The values that a change was and is are left as text, the kind of record behind one being a guess from the name of its field. The names of the staff lines in the Try table of the capacity report are left as text, since following a link would lose what was typed there: for the owner to say. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, asked whether the sweep of P-232 should be finished, which a script had done for the 84 tables of pages: "Yes, in 0.9.8". | 0.9.8 |
| P-241 | **An item is replaced, and takes another's place, more than once.** The rule of D-87 that an item is replaced once and takes the place of one item is lifted. A spare goes into a stack for a member that failed, is pulled later and goes back to the pool, and takes the place of another; and the switch that came back from the manufacturer may take the place of the one that took its own. What stays: an item does not take its own place, and one item does not take the place of the same other twice on one day. Migration `0013` takes from `item_replacements` the two uniquenesses, of the item that left and of the item that came, for an index on each and one uniqueness of the two items with the day. An item's record lists its `replacements`, each with the other item, which of the two this one was, the reason, the reference and the day, the latest first; `replaces` and `replaced_by` stay and are the latest of each, so that the record reads as before. A row of a file of replacements is matched by its HID, or else by its two items and its day; a row that gives no day is the one replacement of those two items where there is one, a new one of today where there is none, and is rejected, asking for the day, where there are several. As built: the same two items the same way on one day are refused in words, with the replacement there is; the same request sent twice is refused the second time as stale, the item that left having moved on by the first. The list of items had no field of a replacement, so no list or report holds an item twice. The way back from migration `0013` holds only where no item was replaced, or took a place, a second time. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, of what the third review had measured and left (O-02): "Agreed on lifting the replace-once rule so replace can be one press, and make it a stack as one press, in 0.9.7 or 0.9.8", which the owner then placed in 0.9.8. In that review a spare that had taken a place once could never be the new item of a replacement again, `409` "this item took the place of another already", which the spares of a pool come to as they go round; and the owner's second common replacement, a member swapped for another model and swapped back, was refused as a loop. | 0.9.8 |
| P-242 | **Replace is one press on a member's row.** The row of a current member of a group has Replace. Its form asks for the item that takes the place, searched for and shown as the picker of members shows a candidate (P-221), the reason and a reference; and, in a stack, what a member that joins and one that leaves are asked: whether the one that comes takes the stack's name and place, the box of P-217, ticked from the start, with Production where it is a spare, and what becomes of the one that left, the choices of P-218. One request does all of it: `POST /items/{hid}/replace` takes `drop_identity`, `disposition` and `expected_online` for the item that comes, as a member added takes them, and `old_item`, what the item that left is to have, as the end of a membership takes `item`. Each is checked by that item's own rules and audited as its change, which says the replacement it came from; where one is refused, nothing is done. The sentence of D-87 that a replacement moves neither item in the lifecycle now reads: a replacement moves neither item by a rule, and changes only what the request names. `drop_identity` is for the replacement of a current member of a stack; what becomes of the old item is given with a day that has come. The page of an item keeps Replace this item, with the same form. As built: a disposition and an intent may be given to the item that comes whether or not the old one is in a group; `drop_identity` needs the item that leaves to be a current member of a stack; with `old_item`, a `replaced_on` to come is refused. Each change of either item says the replacement in `replacing`. What becomes of it is asked on every replacement, in a stack or not, and starts at Nothing now; the forms of a member's row, Change, End and Replace, all stand under the row. Measured again on the inventory of 386 stacks: a failed member replaced in 8 clicks and 23 keys on the stack's page alone, and both members of a stack of two swapped in 22 clicks and 120 keys, nearly all of the keys the new names of the two that go back to the pool. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, of what the third review had measured and left (O-02): "Agreed on lifting the replace-once rule so replace can be one press, and make it a stack as one press, in 0.9.7 or 0.9.8", which the owner then placed in 0.9.8. The owner's two common replacements: a failed switch is pulled and its replacement goes "into the same position", and a member is swapped for another model to add or remove ports. Measured on a scratch inventory of 386 stacks and 1,843 fictional switches, at 1280 by 800: replacing a failed member took some 21 clicks and 29 keys over two item pages, Replace this item standing 6,249 pixels down the page of the item that failed, then the move of the old one to Pending-Removal and the clearing of the new one's name, address and place by hand; swapping both members of a stack of two for another model took some 56 clicks, 128 keys and six item pages. | 0.9.8 |
| P-243 | **A switch that stands alone is made a stack from its page.** An item that may be in a stack and is in none, a major item that is not retired and has not left the team's hands, has Make it a stack beside Join a stack. One request, `POST /items/{hid}/make-stack`, makes the stack and puts the switch into it as its first member, at position 1. The stack is given the name the person confirms, the switch's own from the start, and takes from the switch what a stack holds for its members: the host name and the FQDN, the addresses, each moved to the stack as it is, with its network, its role and whether it is the primary one, and the location, the planned location, the owner and the operator that the switch has of its own. The switch gives each of them up in the same change and has the four it inherits from the stack from then on: nothing is held twice, and nothing is copied down. Its serial number, model, asset tag, state, tags, coverage, registrations and circuits stay its own. The form may name more members, the second switch, each added as P-217 adds one. Every part is audited, the stack made, the membership, each change of the switch and each address moved, and says the stack it was made for. It is refused for a minor item, for one that is retired or has left the team's hands, for one that is in a stack already, and where a group has the name. As built: the switch is the first member as a plain member, not marked as the master that is intended; the name is the item's name, or else its host name, and is asked for where it has neither; an address is moved in one change of the address that names both holders and keeps its HID and its history. Measured again: a stack made of a switch and a spare in 4 clicks and 12 keys on the switch's page alone. | The owner, on 2026-11-18, of what the third review had measured and left (O-02): "Agreed on lifting the replace-once rule so replace can be one press, and make it a stack as one press, in 0.9.7 or 0.9.8", which the owner then placed in 0.9.8. The owner, on 2026-11-17, of how stacks come to be: most "were assembled into a stack during initial setup"; the rest form over time, "a spare or a standalone switch joined to a stack later, or a second switch added to a standalone one, making a stack". Measured in the third review: making a stack of a switch that stood alone took some 35 clicks and 100 keys over four pages, the name, the host name, the FQDN and the place typed again on the new group and cleared on the switch, and the address removed from the switch and added to the stack, since an address cannot change its holder. That the owner and the operator go to the stack with the place is the lead's reading, for the owner to confirm: a stack passes those four to its members. | 0.9.8 |
| P-244 | **The first members of a new stack are offered the box of a member that joins.** In New group, each member chosen for a stack has the box of P-217, ticked from the start, and is asked for Production where it is a spare, as in the form that adds a member to a stack that exists; `POST /device-groups` takes them on each of its `members` already. As built: a member picked while the group is of another type has the box when the type is changed to a stack; a refusal of one member is said beside its row. | P-217 as built: "The first members of a stack made by New group are not offered the box", so that a stack made of two spares kept their names, addresses and depot until each was put right on its own page, eight clicks and three saves each in the third review. | 0.9.8 |
| P-245 | **The condition of a member that leaves is given with the end.** What an item is to have from the end of its membership (P-218), and from its replacement (P-242), may name its `condition`, a value of the list, checked and audited as the item's own. The form's choice To be returned to the manufacturer starts it at Failed, which the person may change; no condition follows from an end by a rule. | P-218 as built: "the condition of the item is not among what the end carries, so that a failed member is marked failed on its own page", 4,190 pixels down it in the third review. The owner, on 2026-11-17: a failed member "is either processed via RMA for replacement by the manufacturer or processed for disposal/excess as bad hardware". | 0.9.8 |
| P-246 | **How a thing that runs on another is held.** A model's `kind` is one of six: `virtual_context`, a virtual device context of a switch or the like of it in a firewall, and `container` join `hardware`, `virtual_machine`, `software` and `service`. The three that run on a host are `virtual_machine`, `virtual_context` and `container`; all three are among the kinds counted from the start, `count_kinds`, so that each is a component and a managed node and carries its weight, as any major item. An item of such a kind has a host: `host_item_hid`, an item, or `host_group_hid`, a stack or a cluster, one or neither and never both, which a person names; and `observed_host_hid` with `host_observed_at`, the item it was seen to run on and when, which is an observation, set by hand or by an import and later by a tool, as the master of a stack is two facts. The record says in `host_differs` when the two differ, which is no fault: a host that is seen to be a current member of the cluster named agrees with it. A host is an item of the kind `hardware` or `virtual_machine`, or with no model, that is not retired and has not left the team's hands, a minor item as well, since a blade is one; never the item itself nor one that runs on it, and the line of hosts is at most three long, a container in a virtual machine on a server. An item of another kind is given no host, and a model is not made another kind while an item of it has one. What runs on a host is a major item: it is installed in nothing. From its host it has, where it has none of its own and its own groups have none, its location, its planned location, its owner and its operator, resolved when read and never copied down: the walk goes from the item to its own groups, to what it runs on, to the groups of that, and so on up, as it goes from a minor item to the item it is in (P-175), and the answer says `host` where the record it runs on holds the value. Its state, its name and its addresses are its own: nothing of its standing comes from its host. A virtual context has no serial number of its own to enter: where it has none, its record shows its host's in `effective.serial_number`, as from its host, which no file matches a row by. `GET /items/{hid}/hosted` and `GET /device-groups/{hid}/hosted` list what runs on a record, and beneath that; the record itself says how many. Migration `0014` adds the four columns to `items`. As built: the two new kinds read Virtual context and Container, and an installation that set `count_kinds` itself keeps what it set. A value says `host` where an item that the item runs on holds it, and `device_group` where a group does, the stack or the cluster it runs on among them, so that the page links to the record that holds it without a guess; each of the four values names that record. Whether a host has left the team's hands is judged by its state in force, a blade with none having its chassis's; a host is checked when it is named; the line of hosts counts the links of hosts alone; naming a host of one kind clears the other; and what an item was seen on is for the kinds that run on a host, set with its moment. Where no item names a host, the step of the walk is left out of every statement, so that an inventory with nothing hosted pays nothing for it. After its review, which found the rules of a host and the walk sound in some 45 shapes and in six trials of two requests at one moment: what an operator has from an item counts what runs on one, so that the page of an operator adds up; a planned place that is opened counts and takes what runs on a host; and a machine that was entered, before 0.9.8, as installed in its server is made to run on it by one change that takes it out and names its host, one press on its page. What the owner then decided of the serial number of a virtual context is P-251, and of a stack P-252. | What P-226 leaves to be chosen when it is built. The owner, on 2026-11-18: "Yes, virtual machines and containers can run on a clusters"; "Values from the host by default to inherit for running-on members include: location, owner, operator"; "intended host and observed host are good for running-on members, as a stack has intended and observed master"; "virtual machines and containers count as components, managed nodes and support weight"; "VDC should show serial number from its host"; and "Can a virtual machine, container, and VDC exist as items without a linked parent? Yes - allow the operators to add items, but items of a kind that should have a parent should indicate this when reviewed." The lead's readings, for the owner to confirm: the planned location is had from the host with the location, since they "move together"; a virtual context is its own kind, where a virtual machine had one already; a host that is a group is a stack or a cluster, the two that are one device; and hardware is given no host. | 0.9.8 |
| P-247 | **What follows from a host.** Retiring an item retires what runs on it, and what runs on that, each with an event of its own that names the host it was retired with; the web lists them first and asks, and `POST /items/retire` takes `dry_run` and says in `hosted` what went, or would go, with the records named. Bringing the host back brings none of them back. Retiring a stack or a cluster that something runs on is refused, naming what runs on it: each is given another host, or retired, first; retiring one host of a cluster leaves what runs on the cluster with the cluster. An item or a group that something runs on, retired or not, is not deleted (P-227), and what was seen to run on a deleted item is cleared of it by an event that says so. A replacement hands what runs on the item that left to the item that came, as it hands over memberships and circuits, unless the request says `hand_over_hosted` false; the form of Replace has a box for it where something runs on the item, ticked from the start: The 3 things that run on it go to the new one. Nothing else follows by a rule: a host that leaves the team's hands, or whose record is retired under an item that was brought back, leaves that item as it is, marked for review (P-249). As built: each item retired with its host says the host in `retired_with`; a dry run changes nothing and answers the same; an item both named and running on one that is named is counted once; the refusal of a stack or a cluster names what runs on it in `depends_on`; a retired item is handed to nobody by a replacement and keeps the host it was retired on; and what is handed over is held to every rule of naming a host, the line of hosts among them. After its review, which found this side sound, 24 trials of two requests at one moment among it: a replacement dated ahead hands nothing over and is refused where something would be, as it changes nothing else of either item before its day; and `hirdir items retire` takes `--dry-run`. | What P-226 leaves to be chosen when it is built. The owner, on 2026-11-18: "When a host is retired, its running-on members should be retired ... possibly with a warning if the action is performed via web interface?"; "VDC cannot exist without its Nexus, stacks are handled different on purpose versus hosted items"; "when hosted on a cluster the virtual machine or container stay with the cluster when a cluster host is retired". Asked, the same day, what should become of twelve virtual machines when the record of their cluster is retired, the owner chose that it be refused, the page listing them; and whether a chassis replaced under RMA hands its virtual contexts to the new one: "Ask in the form", a box ticked from the start, unticked where the replacement is another kind of device that will not host them. That the API hands them over where the request does not say is the lead's reading, as a replacement hands over a position and a circuit. | 0.9.8 |
| P-248 | **A row is found by the tool's own identifier, and what a tool reports no more is marked.** An import of items may name `system`, the external system the file was read from, and its rows then have `external_id`, that system's identifier of each. Such a row is matched first by it: the item that is registered with that system under that identifier, whatever its name is now, so that a rename or a move in the tool makes no second record; where no item is, the row is matched as any row, and the item it finds or makes is registered with the system under the identifier. A row whose item the system knows under another identifier, and an identifier that two items have, is an error on the row, never a guess. Each row matched so says that the system reports the item now: its registration is `registered`, with `confirmed_at` the moment of the import. With `complete`, the file is everything that system reports: once it is applied whole, with no row rejected or left out, every item that is `registered` with the system and that no row named is given the status `not_reported`, with an event each, and is marked for review (P-249). It is never retired or changed otherwise by this, and is `registered` again the next time a file names it. The preview says how many would be marked, and names the first of them. The registrations of device groups are not touched. Which things a tool sends is the tool's to decide: `hirdir` tracks what it is sent. As built: `system` and `complete` are given with the preview, and the file's column is `external_id`. A row that finds its item by its HID is held to the identifier as any row is. A file loaded a second time is unchanged in each row; a row that makes a registration, or brings one back from not reported, removed or pending, is a change of its row and says so in `registration`. Each registration that a file marks has an event that is the import's and names it. Where a file that is everything its system reports marks nothing, the job says why, `not_reported_held`: a row was rejected, left out or stale. The job names the first ten it would mark while it waits to be applied. A person may set the status by hand. What the owner answered when it was reviewed, on 2026-11-18: a row whose identifier finds an item that is retired is rejected, naming it, to be brought back or to have its registration removed, where the review had found the retired record quietly renamed ("Reject the row"); and a file that only confirms what is there moves `confirmed_at` and writes no event for each registration, the import's own event counting how many it confirmed, where the review had counted an event for every row of every night ("One event per import"). And the lead's, told to the owner: a file that is everything its system reports and has no row is refused, so that a report that came out empty marks nothing; one that was cut short still marks what it does not name, which the next whole file undoes. | What P-226 leaves to be chosen when it is built. The owner, on 2026-11-18: "System containers, long-lived, should be tracked much like virtual machines, while short-lived application containers will come and go from the environment ... Consider leaving this for the external system that pushes data into hirdir to be responsible for filtering out short-lived containers", and "Agreed on recommendations for answer 6": what a tool pushes is matched by its identifier in that tool, and an item it reports no more is marked for review and never retired by `hirdir` on its own. Asked the same day how much of it 0.9.8 should build, the lead recommending the matching alone and the mark with discovery after 1.0.0, the owner chose both now. | 0.9.8 |
| P-249 | **What is to be looked at says so.** An item's record has `review`, the reasons it is to be looked at by a person, and none where there is nothing: `no_host`, it is of a kind that runs on a host and names none; `host_gone`, the host it names is retired or has left the team's hands; `not_reported`, a system it is registered with reports it no more (P-248). No reason refuses a change or moves the item: each is a mark, cleared by putting right what it says. The item list filters and shows it, `review`; the page of an item says each reason in words beside what it is about, with the way to put it right; and the first page counts the items to be looked at, as a link to the list of them. As built: each reason has `about`, the host or the system it is about, and for an item that names no host and holds a serial number that one item has, that item (P-251). The reason of an item a system reports no more reads Not reported, as its registration's status does. The first page counts the items to be looked at and, after the count, each reason that counts any, a link to the list of those: the list then says by its filter why each is there. | What P-226 leaves to be chosen when it is built. The owner, on 2026-11-18: "items of a kind that should have a parent should indicate this when reviewed", and that an item a tool reports no more is marked for review. A state is not changed by a rule in `hirdir`: records are flagged for the team to look at. | 0.9.8 |
| P-250 | **A thing that runs on another, on the pages.** Among the first facts of an item of a kind that runs on a host: Runs on, the host as a link, an item or a stack or a cluster, and where one was seen, Seen on, with when, and whether the two differ. Its form has Host, searched for by name as a member is searched for, items and the groups that may host together, and What it was seen on. The page of an item or a group that something runs on lists What runs on it, each as a link with its kind, its state and its address, and what runs on those beneath them. The item list has Runs on as a column to turn on, a link as every value that names a record is (P-232), and finds the items of a host by `host.name`; a bulk edit gives the rows ticked another host, which is how twelve virtual machines are moved before their cluster is retired. Retire on a host says what goes with it before it asks (P-247). The import of items has Read from, a system, and This file is everything it reports (P-248). As built: What runs on it has, beneath its heading, a link to those items in the item list, to be ticked and changed together: twelve machines moved to a cluster in nine presses and the cluster's name. Runs on and To be looked at stand after the columns of a place (P-253), so that the seven the owner asked to see at 1280 pixels stay in view with both on. A model changed to a kind that runs on no host clears, in the same save, what the item ran on and was seen on, and says so beneath the model. After the review of the web page, which found nothing that lost what was typed or did the wrong thing unseen: the message of a retirement counts what the server retired; a refusal goes when what it was about is changed; a new choice of what becomes of an item keeps what was typed; an item that runs on a host is offered no stack to join or to be made; and a switch known by its host name alone is told so before it is made a stack or takes a stack's name, where the server had refused it in words that said nothing of why. | What P-226 leaves to be chosen when it is built. The owner, on 2026-11-18: "List as an item is maybe the most straightforward as it is seen on the network with a management address, but link it with a switch, so they move together" and "How best to support linking one item to another?" | 0.9.8 |
| P-251 | **The serial number given for a virtual context names its host.** A virtual context has no serial number of its own: what a tool reads from it is its switch's. So a serial number given for an item of the kind `virtual_context`, in a row of a file or through the API, is not what the item is found by, and where it finds a host it is not stored: it names the host, as `host` does. Where one item that may be a host has that serial number, that item is the context's host from that change, shown in a preview as any change of a host is; a row that names a host as well, and another item by it, is rejected, and so is a serial number that two items have, never a guess. Where no item has it yet, the context is made, or kept, with the serial number as it was given, held on the context until its host is known, and is marked for review, `no_host`. Nothing joins the two by itself when the switch is entered later: the next report that names the context finds the switch by then and joins them, and until then the page of the context says which item has that serial number, once one has, with Make it its host, one press.
