| P-285 | **A colour is chosen from a grid of 64, by a slider of 32, or typed.** Wherever a colour is entered, an operator's and a tag's, the form has a grid of 64 swatches, eight hues in eight shades, each one press and reached by the keyboard; above it a slider of 32 steps, the four middle shades of each hue in the order of the rainbow, which moves the choice in the grid; and the text box, which takes any `#rrggbb` as now, a value that is not of the grid being kept as typed. The three are one value and stay in step. The value is shown on a chip filled with the colour, its text black or white, whichever is read better on it, and beside it the label as the lists will show it. The 64 and the 32 are fixed in the code, in `hirdir.domain`, and `GET /colours`, for every role, gives them: `grid`, eight rows of eight, `slider`, 32, and `next_for_operators`, the first colour of a fixed order through the 32 that no operator that is not retired has, or the least used where all are taken. What is stored is `#rrggbb` as before, and a colour that is not of the 64 is as good as one that is. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, of the round after 0.9.12: "goal is to improve the daily operator and admin tasks in hirdir, reviewing data, importing, exporting. hirdir must be easy to use, fewer clicks, to encourage teams to maintain their data in the system. data that is easy to update gets updated." The owner's list: "Allow choosing the color for an operator from a color-picker with ~64 as well as allow typing in a color value", "Consider using a slider to select color that selects from at least 32 distinct colors, and show the text in the color, as an example, #eeaa00 in the text box as value for color should show in that color text (somewhat orange)"; asked which form, with the three drawn side by side, the owner chose "Both grid and slider", where the lead had recommended the grid with typing. The chip filled with the colour, in place of text in the colour, is the lead's recommendation, agreed: #eeaa00 as text on a white page is hard to read, and a yellow cannot be read. For tags too, agreed. That the slider rests at the nearest of its 32 steps, its thumb hollow, for a colour that is not one of them, was the lane's choice, told to the owner when it was published, who answered on 2026-11-20: "Agreed on all five calls." | 0.9.13 |
| P-286 | **Every table that is imported is exported from the Import page, an empty one as its template, and all of them at once.** The Import page lists every table a file is taken for, in the order in which files are loaded, each with how many records it holds and Export as CSV and as JSON: the export there is already, `POST /exports`, of the whole table. A table that holds nothing exports its header row and no more as CSV, which is the template for its import; as JSON an empty table is the envelope with no rows, and the page says that the CSV is the template. `GET /exports/tables`, for every role that may export, gives for each table its name, its count and the columns of its file. Export everything is one file, a zip of every table in the order of loading, each as `examples/` names it, CSV or JSON as asked, `POST /exports/all`; the accounts are not in it (P-288). Taking everything out is written to the audit trail, one event with who, when and the format; the export of one table is not, as it never was. **What is exported loads back.** The file of any table, taken as it is by the preview of an import into the inventory it came from, changes nothing: every row is `unchanged`, none rejected. That holds for every table, in both formats, and a test holds it. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, of the round after 0.9.12: "goal is to improve the daily operator and admin tasks in hirdir, reviewing data, importing, exporting. hirdir must be easy to use, fewer clicks, to encourage teams to maintain their data in the system. data that is easy to update gets updated." The owner's list: "Add export for each table that is available for import on the Import page, and a blank table can still be exported to provide the operator with a template to use for import"; and of the lead's two additions, the counts beside each table and one export of everything, and of the rule that an export loads back as nothing to change: "Agreed with recommendations listed, highlight to two things not on list, all good". Asked on 2026-11-20, once it was built, whether exports should be recorded in the audit trail, every one, none, or the whole inventory alone, the owner chose "Export everything only", as the lead recommended: one table is what a list shows and is exported many times a day, the whole inventory seldom. That the list of tables stands beside the upload on the Import page, in a box that scrolls, was the lane's choice, told to the owner when it was published, who answered on 2026-11-20: "Agreed on all five calls." | 0.9.13 |
| P-287 | **Geography is exported as its three tables.** The Geography page has Export, with regions, sites and locations each as CSV and as JSON: the exports there are already, in the columns their imports read, so that a file is corrected and loaded back; an empty table gives its template (P-286). There is no file of the whole tree. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, of the round after 0.9.12: "goal is to improve the daily operator and admin tasks in hirdir, reviewing data, importing, exporting. hirdir must be easy to use, fewer clicks, to encourage teams to maintain their data in the system. data that is easy to update gets updated." The owner's list: "Geography CSV and JSON export"; asked, with the case of twenty shipping addresses corrected in a spreadsheet and loaded back, whether three files, one file of the whole tree or both: "Three files, as imports take them". | 0.9.13 |
| P-288 | **The users are exported as CSV, by an Admin, and not imported.** Admin › Users has Export CSV, `POST /users/export`: a row to each user with `username`, `name`, `email`, `phone`, `phone2`, `role`, `groups`, `operators`, `kind` (person or service), `state` (enabled or disabled), `last_sign_in_at` and `notes`; several groups or operators in one cell as the other files write several values. It holds no access code, no token and no hash of either, ever. The export is an Admin's and is audited as an export is. No file makes or changes a user in this release: making accounts from a file, with how their access codes reach their people, is noted for a later one. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, of the round after 0.9.12: "goal is to improve the daily operator and admin tasks in hirdir, reviewing data, importing, exporting. hirdir must be easy to use, fewer clicks, to encourage teams to maintain their data in the system. data that is easy to update gets updated." The owner's list: "Users CSV export"; asked whether the same file should load users back, to change them or to make them: "Export only for now". | 0.9.13 |
| P-289 | **A person who signs in changes their own profile, on a page of their own.** The name of who is signed in, in the menu bar beside the mark of health and the role, is a link to their profile. There they change their own name, email address and first and second number, and their organization, which are their contact's; the label and the colour of their operator, where they are one; and their own access code. The particulars are asked of the owner before it is built: what a person with no contact or behind several operators is shown, what changing a code asks for and ends, and which role may do what a role could not do to another's record. Every such change is role-checked as the person's own and audited. It is built with P-291, as the round of the person. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, while 0.9.13 was built: "For the next round before 1.0.0, advise if more than one round is needed. Allow users that login to modify their profile, change their own display name, email address, first and second phone number, change their operator label if the user account is also an operator, change their operator color if the user account is also an operator, change their organization if the user is also a contact, all on a profile page, and allow the user to change their access code on the profile page. The user can access their profile page by clicking on their name displayed in the upper rigt of the display by the health icon and role." Until now an Admin alone changes an account and sets a code, and a contact is changed by the Inventory role. Asked at the start of 0.9.14, on 2026-11-20, the owner answered three of its particulars. Whether a person with the View-only role changes their own name, email, numbers and organization, and their own label and colour: "Yes, everything of their own". Whether a code that a person chooses has a least length: "A setting, 12 from the start". And what the page offers a person whose account has no contact: "Say so, and whom to ask". The routes and the page that follow are P-294 and P-295. | 0.9.14 |
| P-290 | **The words the pages show are gone over for plain writing, after 1.0.0.** Every statement that hirdir displays is compared with the Army's rules of effective writing and with ASD-STE100, Simplified Technical English: concise, in the active voice, and plain enough that a person with an eighth to tenth grade education understands it quickly. A title of one word is good. Where statements are listed, the longer descriptions are shortened and the words of each sentence counted down. It is a round of its own: a list of the words and forms to use is agreed first, then the pages, the messages of the server and the scenarios that read them are changed together. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, while 0.9.13 was built: "For the roadmap to consider after 1.0.0, review displayed text and compare to lessons learned from Army effective writing style and asdste-100.com, where written statements must be concise, active tense, straightforward for an individual with an 8th to 10th grade education to quickly understand the statement; apply this writing style to the statements displayed in hirdir. One word titles are good, where statements are listed consider adjustments that can shorten the longer descriptions and reduce word count in sentences." | after 1.0.0 |
| P-291 | **The user, the contact and the operators of one person are retired together, or parted first.** The three are linked already, by HID: a user names its contact and an operator names its contact, so the contact is what joins them, and no field is added. The page of each shows the chain, operator, contact, user, each a link. Retiring one of them says first what it is linked to and proposes the rest in one confirmation: the user disabled, the contact retired, and for each operator a choice, handed over to another person, left open or released, its label being on equipment still. A retirement that says nothing of the linked records is refused, with the linked records named, so that nothing changes without being said and nothing is left half done; the way on is to do them together or to part them first. All of one confirmation is one transaction, each record audited. Until it is built, a user whose contact is retired is found in the gaps of the Users list (P-280). The particulars are asked of the owner before it is built. | The review of the server of 0.9.13 found that a user whose contact is retired was in no gap. Asked on 2026-11-20, with the case of an engineer who leaves, whether a retired contact counts as none, the owner answered with a design: "There should be a link between the three objects (email address, HID or other field?) where retiring one object should retire the linked objects - user, operator, contact - should travel together, with a confirmation. Admin moves to retire one, hirdir suggests retire the linked objects, or first divide the objects to break the links. Rufusing one that doesn't take into account disposition for the linked objects is advied or no? Maybe show that clearly on the page for each, operator linked to contact linked to user." The lead advised that a bare retirement be refused, with the linked records named, since the owner would rather be asked than have a state changed by a rule; and, asked where it goes, the owner chose "0.9.14, with the profile page". Asked at the start of 0.9.14, on 2026-11-20, with the cases of somebody who moves to another department, whose account is retired while they are still whom to call for a site, and of somebody who stays and looks after equipment no longer, whose label alone is retired, whether the Admin may keep some of the linked records in the one confirmation, the owner chose "Yes, each a choice, retire ticked": each linked record is listed with Retire ticked from the start, the Admin unticks what stays, and one rule holds, that a contact is not retired while its account or a label still stands on it. What a request says of the linked records, and what is refused, is P-297. | 0.9.14 |
| P-292 | **A user account is retired, which is other than disabled, and the list of users leaves the retired out until they are asked for.** A user is active, disabled or retired. Disabled is an account that may not sign in for now and is to come back; retired is the account of somebody who has left, kept for the audit trail and for what it did, and never signed in with again. Admin › Users shows the active and the disabled, each said to be which in words, and a box or a link beside the list brings in the retired, or shows them by themselves, as Include archived does on the lists of the inventory. Until now a user was only enabled or disabled, with no way to put an account away; this is the state of the user that the retirement of a person together reaches (P-291). It needs a migration. The particulars are asked of the owner before it is built: whether a retired account is brought back and by whom, what becomes of its username, its groups, its grants and its service tokens, and where else a retired user is left out. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, while 0.9.13 was built: "Another action for 0.9.14, a state other than Active and Disabled for Users, some way to handle the User account as retired versus disabled, clearly shown when viewing the list of users, but not cluttering the view with retired accounts by default, such as the User list under Admin section shows the Active and Disabled user accounts, and a checkbox or link adds to the view or shows a different view of the archived, retired user accounts, such as for employees that departed the organization. If this is already available it wasn't clear to the production team on viewing the Users page and Admin section." It was not available: `users.disabled` is all there is. And on the same day, of the questions the lead listed for the start of 0.9.14: "handle retired account as with other retired objects, can an Admin bring something from retirement back into active status? If anything from retirement can be brought back, it requires an Admin, and only Admin for user accounts as only an Admin can create a user, grant permissions, and ultimately retire a user account. Related question - if an account is retired, can a new account be created that overlaps such as same username, same email address, as a retired account? Open to to recommendations on how best to handle. ... Retired user accounts must not be shown in active context views for pickers, the normal users' CSV as that should match the users' view which doesn't shown retired users." So: a retired account is brought back by an Admin and by nobody else; it is in no picker and not in the users' file unless the retired are asked for; and whether a new account may take the username of a retired one is still to be settled, the lead recommending that the name stay the retired account's, a new account of that name refused with the retired one named and Bring back offered, since the audit trail is read by username. Asked at the start of 0.9.14, on 2026-11-20, the owner settled the rest. Whether an Admin can rename a retired account the owner asked first; told that an Admin renames any account today, a disabled one too, and that the audit trail keeps who did a thing by HID and shows the username the account has now, so that two people are never shown under one name, the owner chose "Yes, kept until renamed": a retired account keeps its username, a new account of that name is refused with the retired one named, and an Admin who wants the name for somebody new renames the retired account first. Who brings back what is retired: "Admin for people, Inventory for the rest", a user, a contact and an operator by an Admin alone, and equipment, places, contracts and the rest as before (P-298). And what becomes of the groups and the role of an account that is retired: "Taken away, and recorded", as a retired switch leaves its stacks; brought back, the account has no role and no group until an Admin gives them, with a new access code. The particulars are P-296. | 0.9.14 |
| P-293 | **What is typed or pasted is put into one form, by formats an Admin turns on and defines, after 1.0.0.** Where a person types or pastes a value into the web page, a format may tidy it: a telephone number taken as its digits and written in the pattern chosen, `###-###-####` or `+1 (###) ###-####` or one an Admin defines for another country or another telephone system; an email address in lower case and of the form `name@some.domain`; a name in Title Case. Each format is a setting an Admin turns on or off. Pasting is never hindered: the value is taken as it is pasted and then written again in the format where one applies. Every place where data is typed or pasted is looked at for whether a format would keep it clean. To be settled when it is taken up: that the server applies the same format to what comes by a file, the API and the command line, or only the web page does; what becomes of the values held already; and the names a rule of capitals would spoil; the lead recommended that the server apply the format to all of them, that what is held be put right in one pass with a preview or as each is saved, and that Title Case be a format that tidies and gives way to a person's own correction, and the owner agreed. An email address is held to its form alone, in lower case: any domain is taken, one that does not exist too, `john.smith@localhost` as `john.smith@example.com`. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, while 0.9.13 was built: "For the roadmap after 1.0.0, input filters and pattern enforcement with toggles as Admin settings, such as phone numbers ###-###-#### versus +1 (###) ###-#### and flexible for an admin to define their own for other locales, other phone systems, where the web interface takes the digits and applies the filter, don't disrupt the ability for a user to paste a value into a text box but do re-format the value where applicable. Email address format to enforce lower case with @some.domain suffix. Name enforced as Title Case. Review where data is typed or copy-pasted by a user into a browser and consider an option to enable an input-filter/formatter to keep the data clean." And later that day: "Agreed on recommendations for P-293. No restriction on which domain is used for email, even a bogus domain is OK, so long as the format is correct, e.g., john.smith@localhost and john.smith@example.com". | after 1.0.0 |
| P-294 | **The profile is the person's own records, changed by routes of their own.** `GET /auth/me` answers, beside the user, `contact`, the contact the person is with its `name`, `email`, `phone`, `phone2`, `organization` and `row_version`, or null, and `operators`, every operator that is not retired whose contact is theirs, each with `hid`, `label`, `colour` and `row_version`: a person behind several labels sees and changes each. `PATCH /auth/me/contact` takes `row_version` and any of `name`, `email`, `phone`, `phone2` and `organization`, and nothing else of a contact, not its notes, its tags or its state. `PATCH /auth/me/operators/{hid}` takes `row_version` and either of `label` and `colour`, of an operator that is theirs and not retired, and nothing else, not who is behind it. Each is held to the rules of `PATCH /contacts/{hid}` and of `PATCH /operators/{hid}`, an email or a label that another has being refused with that other named, and each is audited as that change is, by the person. Every role does this for its own records, View-only too, and nobody for another's: there is no HID of a user or of a contact in these routes to send. A label is changed with no further guard: what the label is on holds the operator by HID, and a file written with the old label no longer finds it, which is so of any change of a label. A person with no contact is told so on the page, with the words of whom to ask (P-169), and is offered their access code alone: an Admin gives an account its contact. A service account has no profile, and these routes refuse a token with a `403`. The page is `/profile`, reached from the name in the menu bar, and the CLI has `hirdir profile`, which shows it and changes it. | The lead's particulars of P-289, from the owner's answers of 2026-11-20. Routes of the person's own, with no HID in them, cannot be turned on another's record, and leave what a role may do to the rest of the inventory as it was. Readings made while it was built, for the owner to confirm: the three routes of a person's own need a session and no role, as `GET /auth/me` does, so that somebody whose account was just brought back, and holds no role yet, changes the code an Admin gave them and their own details; `GET /auth/me` answers no `record_status`, since whoever reads it is signed in and so is never retired; the menu bar shows the username, as before, as the link to the profile, with the person's name in the link's title; the page of an account with no role names no least length until a code is refused and takes a colour as typed, the settings and the colours being read with a role; and each part of the page has a button of its own, named for what it saves. The review found that any account may now give its contact a name that another contact has, as the Inventory role always could, after which a file that names that contact by its name alone is rejected and asks for the email address or the HID: that is left as it is. | 0.9.14 |
| P-295 | **A person changes their own access code with the code they have, and a typed code has a least length.** `POST /auth/me/access-code` takes `current_access_code` and `access_code`. A wrong current code is refused with a `422` on `current_access_code`, counted against the person as a failed sign-in is, so that a session left open is no way to try codes, and written to the audit trail. The new code ends every other session of the person and the answer carries a new session token for the one in hand, which stays signed in. The setting `access_code_min_length`, 12 from the start, from 1 to 64, is the least length of a code that is typed, by the person here and by an Admin on `POST /users`, `POST /users/{hid}/access-code` and `hirdir admin`; a shorter one is a `422` on `access_code` that says the length. A code the server makes, three groups of four, is not held to it. Codes that are held already are not looked at: nobody is signed out by the setting. Which characters a code has is left free, and the rest of the code rules stay for later (R12.4). | The owner, on 2026-11-20, asked whether a code a person chooses should have a least length, today one character being accepted: "A setting, 12 from the start". That the current code is asked for and the other sessions ended was told to the owner in the same question. Readings made while it was built, for the owner to confirm: a new code may be the same as the current one, nothing saying that it must differ; an attempt made while the person is delayed is audited as `access_code_delayed`, as `sign_in_delayed` is beside `sign_in_failed`; the right current code clears the count of wrong ones though the new code is then refused; and the refusal of a short code says the length in words, "at least 12 characters", with no name of a setting in them. | 0.9.14 |
| P-296 | **A retired user is a record status, and retiring takes away what the account could do.** A user has `record_status`, `active` or `retired`, beside `disabled`, which stays what it was: an account that may not sign in for now. `POST /users/{hid}/retire`, for an Admin, ends every session, erases the access code, takes the account out of each of its groups and revokes each role granted to the account itself, and revokes the tokens of a service account, each written to the audit trail as it is when done by itself, and is refused where it would leave no Admin. A retired account signs in with nothing, is refused as an unknown one is, and is given no code, token, group or role while it is retired. Its username, its notes and its contact taken off are all that `PATCH /users/{hid}` changes of it. `GET /users` leaves the retired out; `retired=yes` brings them in and `retired=only` gives them alone, and the users' file takes the same and has a `record_status` column. A retired account is in no gap. `POST /users/{hid}/bring-back`, for an Admin, makes it active again with a new access code, shown once, no role and no group. The username stays the retired account's: a new account or a rename to it is refused with a `409` that says the account is retired and names it, and the Admin renames the retired one to free the name. The list of who acted, on the Audit page, still offers a retired account, marked, since what it did is still there; it is an Admin's, as the list of users is, and an event answers the HID and the username of who acted and not their state. Migration `0017` adds the column and changes no record. | The lead's particulars of P-292, from the owner's answers of 2026-11-20. A column named as on every other record keeps one word for retired through the inventory, and leaves `disabled` to mean what it did, so that no file, script or page that reads it is broken. Readings made while it was built, for the owner to confirm: the contact of a retired account stays that account's, and is refused to another user with the retired account named, until it is taken off; `retired=only` with `contact=some` lists a retired account that has a contact; retiring leaves `disabled` as it was and bringing back clears it; an Admin retires their own account where another Admin remains, is told so before, and is signed out; the contact of an account that is retired or brought back has an `update` event of its own, `user_record_status`, so that its history says when it stopped being a user's; the Users list keeps the name Every user for the list without the retired, with words above it that say so, beside Retired users; and Who, on the Audit page, is new with this release. | 0.9.14 |
| P-297 | **A retirement says what becomes of each linked record, in `linked`.** `GET /users/{hid}/linked`, `GET /contacts/{hid}/linked` and `GET /operators/{hid}/linked`, which every role reads, answer the person a record belongs to: `user`, `contact` and `operators`, each with its HID, what it is known by and its `record_status`, a contact and an operator with `uses`, how much names it. The retire route of each of the three takes `linked` beside `row_version`: `user` and `contact`, each `retire` or `keep`, and `operators`, each with `hid` and `action`, `retire`, which releases what the label is on, `keep`, `open`, which leaves the label with nobody behind it, or `hand_over` with `contact_hid`, the person it goes to. Every linked record that is not retired is to be said, other than the one the route is on: where one is not, nothing changes and the answer is a `409` that names each. A contact is not retired while its account or a label stands on it: with the contact retired, the user is `retire` and each operator `retire`, `open` or `hand_over`, or the request is a `422`. A request that retires a user is an Admin's; one that retires a contact or an operator and keeps the user is the Inventory role's, as before. All of it is one transaction, each record audited as if it were done by itself. A record with nothing linked is retired as before, with no `linked`. An import does not retire: a row of a file that would retire a contact or an operator is rejected as a row that would retire any record is, so the rule has nothing to hold in a file. The CLI has `hirdir users retire`, `hirdir contacts retire` and `hirdir operators retire`, which print what is linked and take what becomes of each as options. The page of each of the three shows the chain, operator, contact, user, each a link, a retired one marked. | The lead's particulars of P-291, from the owner's answer of 2026-11-20, "Yes, each a choice, retire ticked". That each linked record is said, kept ones too, holds the owner's rule that nothing changes, and nothing is left, without being said. Readings made while it was built, for the owner to confirm: retiring an operator that has a contact says what becomes of that contact, and of its user and its other operators, kept ones too, so a script that released a label with its version alone now sends `linked`; somebody of the Inventory role cannot retire a contact that is a user's, since the user goes with it and that is an Admin's; a refusal names an operator by its label, a contact by its name and a user by its username; on the pages the button that confirms says what it will do as the ticks stand, as Release KL only or Release KL and retire 2 more, and a confirmation whose linked records change while it is open reads them again and says so; and an import does not retire, as before, so that no row of a file has anything to say of linked records. | 0.9.14 |
| P-298 | **A retired user, contact or operator is brought back by an Admin alone.** Until now the Inventory role brought back a contact or an operator by setting its `record_status`; from 0.9.14 that change, from `retired`, is an Admin's, on the API, in a file and in the CLI, and is a `403` for anybody else, while retiring one stays the Inventory role's. Every other kind of record is brought back as before. `POST /users/{hid}/bring-back` takes `linked` as a retirement does: `contact`, `bring_back`, which a user whose contact is retired must say or have its contact taken off first, since an account does not stand on a retired contact; and `operators`, each with `hid` and `bring_back`, for those of that contact that are retired, which nothing obliges: a label may have gone to somebody else since. A label brought back is on everything it was on, since what holds a label keeps it. A label is not brought back onto a retired contact: by itself it is refused with the contact named, until the contact is brought back or taken off the label. | The owner, on 2026-11-20, asked whether "it requires an Admin, and only Admin for user accounts" meant every record: "Admin for people, Inventory for the rest". Decided by the lead after the review of the server, as the safest reading of the owner's rule, for the owner to confirm: a retired operator is not brought back onto a contact that is still retired, by `PATCH /operators/{hid}` or by a row of a file; it is a `409` on `record_status` that names the contact, and the way on is to bring the contact back first or to take it off the label, which then comes back open. The lane had first read it the other way. | 0.9.14 |

| ID | Question | Needed by |
|---|---|---|
| O-01 | Which monitoring system is integrated first, and does `hirdir` send to it or read from it? | after 1.0.0 |
| O-02 | Answered, and built in 0.9.8: the owner agreed on 2026-11-18 to lift the rule that an item is replaced, and takes another's place, once (D-87), so that Replace is one press on a member's row of a stack (P-241, P-242), and to making a stack of a switch that stands alone in one press (P-243). What the owner raised the same day, how a thing that runs inside another, a virtual device context in a switch, a virtual machine on a hypervisor, a container, is an item linked to its host and moves with it, is P-226, with its particulars P-246 to P-252. Nothing of it is open. | 0.9.8 |
